# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Commands

```bash
# Development
pnpm dev              # Build packages, then run api + web concurrently
pnpm dev:web          # Build packages, run web only
pnpm dev:api          # Build packages, run api only

# Build
pnpm build            # Build all packages and apps

# Quality
pnpm lint             # ESLint check (max-warnings 0)
pnpm lint:fix         # ESLint auto-fix
pnpm format           # Prettier write all files
pnpm typecheck        # Real `tsc --noEmit` across apps/web, apps/api, packages/ui (strict — reports all errors)
pnpm validate         # lint + typecheck (full check — run manually or in CI)

# Pre-commit hook runs `pnpm lint` only. `pnpm typecheck` is strict and the web
# app still carries pre-existing TS errors, so it is NOT in the pre-commit hook
# (it would block every commit). Run `pnpm typecheck` to find them and clean up;
# once the web app is clean, restore `pnpm run validate` in `.husky/pre-commit`.

# Docker (PostgreSQL 16, Redis 7, pgAdmin)
pnpm docker:up        # Start services
pnpm docker:down      # Stop services
pnpm docker:logs      # Follow logs
pnpm docker:clean     # Remove containers and volumes
```

Packages must be built before apps can run (`pnpm dev` handles this automatically). `apps/api` has a **minimal Jest harness** for pure-logic helpers only: `pnpm --filter api test` (config at `apps/api/jest.config.js`, CJS + ts-jest, with a `@repo/shared → dist/cjs` moduleNameMapper and a `uuid` CJS shim for uuid@13 ESM). Covered: `profit-calculation.ts`, `order-matcher.ts`, `pick-best-match.ts`, `amazon-order-parser.service.ts`, `google-link-decision.ts`, `password-reset-helpers.ts`. DB/queue/NestJS layer is still manual-verified — integration tests are deferred (deliberate, not a gap). `apps/web` has no tests yet.

## Architecture

**pnpm workspace monorepo** with two apps and three packages:

| Path | Description |
|---|---|
| `apps/api` | NestJS 10.3+ backend. Raw PostgreSQL via `pg` (no ORM). BullMQ job queues (Redis). JWT auth (Passport). |
| `apps/web` | React 18 + Vite 5. Redux Toolkit + RTK Query. Emotion CSS-in-JS. i18n (EN + TR). |
| `packages/shared` | Domain types, DTOs, Zod schemas, constants, i18n resources. Dual-format build (ESM + CJS). |
| `packages/ui` | Design system (Atomic Design). Theme tokens, styled components, contexts. |
| `packages/mcp` | Stub — no source code yet. |

### Key Patterns

**Container/Component split** — Every page:
- `[Feature]Page.container.tsx` — Logic, RTK Query hooks, state management
- `[Feature]Page.component.tsx` — Presentation only (only `useTranslation` hook allowed)
- `[Feature]Page.style.ts` — Emotion styled components
- `[Feature]Page.types.ts` — Feature-specific types

**API calls** — All frontend API interactions use RTK Query via `baseApi.injectEndpoints`. No direct fetch/axios.

**Shared types** — All domain types/DTOs/interfaces in `packages/shared/src/domain/`. All Zod schemas in `packages/shared/src/schemas/`. No duplicates, no `any`.

**Database** — Raw `pg` driver via `DatabaseService` (query, getClient, transaction methods). Schema in `docker/postgres/init.sql`. No ORM.

**i18n** — No hardcoded UI strings. Files in `packages/shared/src/i18n/resources/{en,tr}/`. Key resolution rule:
- Domain namespace JSONs (`ebay.json`, `dashboard.json`, `orders.json`, etc.) have a **top-level key matching the namespace name** (e.g. `{ "ebay": { "connect": { "title": "..." } } }`).
- When calling `t()` with the namespace as default (via `useTranslation(['ebay', 'translation'])`), use **dot notation with the full path including the wrapper key**: `t('ebay.connect.title')`. Do NOT use colon syntax — `t('ebay:connect.title')` will show the raw key.
- Cross-namespace access to `translation` (which has no wrapper key) uses **colon syntax**: `t('translation:common.loading')`.
- Summary: dot notation (`t('ebay.connect.title')`) for the primary namespace, colon syntax (`t('translation:key')`) only for cross-namespace access to `translation`.

**Reference module** — `store-settings` is the canonical pattern:
- Backend: `apps/api/src/modules/store-settings/`
- Frontend: `apps/web/src/features/store-settings/`
- Shared: `packages/shared/src/domain/store-settings/`
- **`amazonTaxRate`** (`store_settings.amazon_tax_rate`, migration `035`): per-user global percent 0–100, default `0`. User-configurable in Settings → StoreSettingsDrawer. Used by `OrderSyncService.recomputeProfit` to estimate provisional order profit when the real Amazon tax is unknown (see "Net Profit Formula" below). The Save handler writes the column for ALL of the user's `store_settings` rows (global + per-store) so the resolved value is consistent everywhere — the upsert path must NOT skip the column on the `UPDATE` branch.

### The seller address is ONE address, four fields (2026-09-02)

`StoreSettingsDrawer` asked for the address **twice** — a `country`/`state`/`zip` block feeding the eBay inventory location, and a nested bordered "ship-from" card (name, phone, street ×2, city) feeding the tracking provider's profile. It always WAS one address: `AquilineProfileService.resolveShipFromAddress` composed its payload from *both* groups. Sellers correctly read it as being asked the same thing twice.

**Now: one `FieldGroup` inside the form card, four fields — Country, Region/State, City, Zip — and all four are required** (`isContinueDisabled` blocks the step; `isStoreAddressComplete` in `packages/shared` is the single predicate).

**The requirement is eBay's alone — the tracking provider needs NO address (verified live 2026-09-02).** `POST /v1/profiles` with `{"accountOrigin":"amazon"}` and nothing else returns 200 and stores `storeAddress: null`; `accountOrigin` is its only required field. So `AquilineProfileService` sends no `storeAddress`, `fingerprintProfile` no longer hashes one, and its old gate — which refused to create a profile (and therefore blocked ALL conversion) until a synthesized address was complete — is deleted. Full evidence, including that `DELETE /v1/profiles/{id}` really is unsupported and that the probe cost one permanent slot, is in `docs/aquiline-open-questions.md` question 5.

| | eBay `STORE` location | Aquiline profile |
|---|---|---|
| addressLine1 | required | not sent |
| city | required | not sent |
| stateOrProvince | required | not sent |
| postalCode | required | not sent |
| country | required | not sent |
| name / phone / line2 | not used | not sent |

- **`ship_from_city` is reused as the location city.** Migration `089` minted it for the deleted separate form; the values in it are already cities, so reusing the column beat a migration that only renames. Its wire name (`shipFromCity`) is unchanged — see the comment on `StoreSettings.shipFromCity` for why. `ship_from_name`/`phone`/`address_line1`/`address_line2` are **dormant**: no UI writes them, nothing reads them, not dropped (standing practice). Do not reintroduce inputs for them without re-reading why the two-address drawer was merged.
- **`inheritMissingStoreLocation` inherits `shipFromCity`** along with country/state/zip. A store row created by a focused drawer (blacklist, buyer messaging) has no city of its own, and without inheritance it would publish an eBay item location with an empty city while the global row has one — the same bug the postcode line there already existed to prevent.
- **No street is collected, and one is DERIVED for eBay.** A dropshipper has no warehouse, so a street would be a field with no true value they must invent. eBay requires one, so `buildStoreStreetLine` (pure, shared, Jest-covered) returns `"Sheridan, WY"` from city + region. This replaced two real defects that had been live for the whole life of `ensureInventoryLocation`: `addressLine1` fell back to the literal string **`'Use Store Address'`** (`ListingCreationData.address1` was never populated by any caller, so that placeholder reached eBay as every seller's street), and `city` was fed from `data.location`, which is the **state** — a seller in Sheridan, WY published an item location of "WY, WY". eBay publishes only city/region/country to buyers, so the derived line is never rendered on a listing.
- **Per-store address overrides are kept** — a seller may set a different address per eBay store, and the eBay inventory location is per store. Nothing has to reconcile several addresses into one provider profile any more, because no address reaches the provider.
- **Never tell a seller this address is buyer-visible on the provider's side.** The old copy called it "the return address shown on converted tracking" — **our own assumption**, propagated from design notes into seller-facing i18n, never a provider statement. It is moot now (no address is sent) but the lesson is not: seller-facing copy must claim only what is verified. What IS verified is the eBay half — the item location, of which buyers see city/region/country only.
- The old copy also said an incomplete address makes conversion "fall back to the raw Amazon number, so your supplier stays visible". That was **stale** — it described the rule reversed on 2026-09-01. `mayPushToEbay` holds the order instead; see "THE RAW AMAZON TRACKING NUMBER IS NEVER PUSHED TO eBAY".
- **A deep-linked drawer now adopts its config when the query resolves.** `StoreSettingsDrawer` is always mounted with `isOpen` as a prop, so `/settings?drawer=storeSettings` has it TRUE on the first render — before the settings query returns. The `didOpen` reset therefore never fired, the form initialised from an empty config and never re-synced, and the seller saw a blank address they had actually saved. Latent while every field was optional; making all four required turned it into a disabled Continue plus a "fill all four" warning shown to someone already complete. The container now also resyncs on the `null → loaded` transition of the resolved config (`configArrived`) — deliberately only that transition, so the new object a post-save refetch hands back cannot clobber edits.

**Landing page** (`apps/web/src/features/landing/`) — public marketing page at `/` (no locale prefix). **Light, clean SaaS design (rebuilt 2026-08-12), dark-navy sellerboard-format hero (2026-08-13 — see below)**, TR/EN. **Forced light-only, no dark mode (2026-08-13)** — `LandingPageContainer` wraps its return in a nested `EmotionThemeProvider theme={lightTheme}` (`@emotion/react` + `@repo/ui`'s exported `lightTheme`), which overrides the app-wide theme context (visitor's OS/localStorage preference) for every styled-component and `Icon` under it, without touching the authenticated app's own light/dark support. There is no `ThemeToggle` anywhere on the page (removed from both the navbar and the footer) — a marketing page toggling modes was extra chrome sellerboard itself doesn't have. The per-theme token values the rest of this section describes (`heroBg`, `sectionDeep`, etc.) still exist and still resolve correctly under the forced light theme; they simply never see their dark branch rendered here anymore.
- Container (`LandingPage.container.tsx`) owns all state/logic: scroll detection, mobile menu, locale switching, navigation to `/{locale}/login|register`, and entering the demo. Component (`LandingPage.component.tsx`) is presentation-only (`useTranslation` + the scroll-reveal `IntersectionObserver`, which is a visual concern).
- **The Hero *section* is dark-navy again (2026-08-13) — read this carefully, it has flipped twice.** The rest of the page (`Page` background, every other `Section`) still follows the theme exactly as the paragraph below always said: `colors.landing.heroText`/`heroTextMuted` are real ink (dark in light theme, light in dark theme) and must NOT be used on a brand-coloured fill; text/icons on a brand fill use `colors.landing.onAccent` (always light, both themes). But the `<S.Hero>` element itself (and the fixed `<S.Navbar>` above it) now paints `colors.sidebar.background` — the app's own sidebar colour, deep blue in light theme / near-black in dark theme — **not** `colors.landing.heroBg`, to match sellerboard's permanent dark-navy top band ("üst taraf mavi, alt beyaz" — blue top, white bottom). `HeroTitle`/`HeroSubtitle`/`HeroNote`/`HeroNoteTitle`/`HeroNoteLine` are Hero-exclusive, so they were repointed straight to `colors.sidebar.text`/`textMuted`/`divider`/`hover` — safe because nothing else renders them on a light surface. `NavLink`/`NavDropdownTrigger`/`Hamburger` got the same unconditional treatment (Navbar-only). Components reused on a **light** surface elsewhere (`LoginButton`, `LanguageTrigger`, `LanguageText` — also rendered in the light `MobileMenu` panel and the light `Footer`) instead got a `$onDark` boolean prop, set only at their Navbar call site; omit it and they fall back to the original theme-following `landing.*` colours. **The dark hero cuts directly to the light page below it, no gradient (2026-08-13, final call after two tries at a soft blend)** — `HeroFade` (a transparent→`landing.heroBg` overlay, tried at two different heights/easings to fix a pale patch it left under the trial note) is deleted outright; sellerboard's own hero is a hard blue-to-white edge with no fade at all, and `Hero`'s solid `sidebar.background` butting straight against `Page`'s `heroBg` already produces that line with no extra element needed. The animated aurora panel behind the auth pages (`MeshBackground`) draws white particles and must stay dark, so it has its own **`colors.landing.auroraBg`**, independent of both `heroBg` and the Hero section's `sidebar.background` fill — still never merge any of these.
- Styled with Emotion `styled` + `tkn()` tokens from `colors.landing.*` (`heroBg`, `auroraBg`, `onAccent`, `shadowSoft`, `shadowStrong`, `heroGlow`, `heroGlowAlt`, `heroText`, `heroTextMuted`, `heroBorder`, `cardBorder`, `cardBorderHover`, `chipBg`, `chipBorder`, `sectionAlt`, `sectionDeep`, `heroGradient`, etc.). When adding new landing tokens, register them in **all three** places: `theme.types.ts` (`ThemeColors.landing`), `themes.ts` (light + dark blocks), and `tkn.ts` (`ThemePath` union) — otherwise `tkn('colors.landing.x')` is a type error.
- **Three things carry the "premium" read, and each replaced something that looked amateur:** (1) the hero background is layered radial washes, **never a ruled grid** — the previous 1px repeating linear-gradients read as graph paper behind the headline; (2) elevation uses `shadowSoft`/`shadowStrong`, which are **brand-tinted** in the light theme, because a neutral grey drop-shadow on a near-white page is what makes a layout look like a template (dark theme uses real black — a coloured glow on near-black reads as haze); (3) `heroBg` is `#FBFCFE`, a hair off pure white, so `surface.primary` cards visibly lift off the page. Display type is deliberately restrained (hero maxes at 3.4rem, section titles 2.15rem) — oversized headings were the other half of the template look.
- **The locale control is the app's, not a second dialect of it.** The navbar uses a `Dropdown` with a trigger matching `AppShell.style.ts`'s `LanguageSelectTrigger`, not the `LanguageSwitcher` molecule's segmented `EN|TR` pair — a visitor who signs up should meet the same control they just used. **The trigger shows the full language name, not a 2-letter code (2026-08-13, both here and in the app)** — `Türkçe ⌄` / `English ⌄`, matching sellerboard's own trigger. Previously both `LandingPage`'s `S.LanguageText` and `AppShell.style.ts`'s `LanguageText` rendered an uppercased 2-letter code (`i18nLanguage.toUpperCase()` in the app, `currentLocale.slice(0, 2)` on the landing) with `text-transform: uppercase` baked into the style; both now resolve `t(\`translation:languages.${locale}\`)` (the same key the dropdown's own item list already used) and the uppercase transform is removed from both `LanguageText` styles — forcing it on a real word like "Türkçe" reads wrong the way it never did on a 2-letter code. The `Dropdown`'s own `width` grew `6.25rem → 8rem` at all four call sites (`AppLayout`, `OperatorLayout`, landing navbar, landing footer) to fit the longer labels.
- **Positioning: a recognizable Amazon-to-eBay automation platform first, differentiated second (content rewritten 2026-09-06).** The page deliberately competes IN the AutoDS / Easync / Yaballe category rather than claiming a new one — an experienced dropshipper must think "this is an Amazon-to-eBay automation tool, and the pricing is attractive", never "what is this?". So the copy uses the category's own vocabulary (automatic order fulfillment, price & stock monitoring, tracking automation, automated buyer messages, AI-powered listings) instead of avoiding standard terms to sound unique.
  - **The word "dropshipping" itself is banned from seller-facing copy (2026-09-07, operator decision).** Every other term in this section stays; this one word does not appear in any string a seller or visitor can read — landing, auth, template descriptions, anywhere in `packages/shared/src/i18n/resources/**`. The category is carried by "Amazon → eBay automation" instead, which reads the same to someone who knows AutoDS/Easync/Yaballe. Note this is the ONLY vocabulary restriction: automatic order fulfillment, price & stock sync and the rest are still the right words. The term remains fine in *internal* prose — this file, code comments about the fulfillment flow — because nothing there is rendered to a user. Before adding marketing copy, grep the i18n resources for it.
  - **The earlier copy inverted this and read as a different product.** It led on abstractions ("Run your store on autopilot", "One flow, not five tools", "Nothing invented", "Your rules. Per product, not per account.") and never named the category, so the hero could be read as a profit-analytics tool rather than a dropshipping automation platform. The previous revision of this bullet also justified that framing with "no dropshipping competitor reports profit with cost confidence" — a claim strong enough to shape the whole page and **not worth resting on**: Easync/AutoDS/Yaballe are the comparison set, and the honest position is parity on automation plus a better price plus two genuinely strong features, not category invention.
  - **Three things carry the argument, in this order**: (1) the automation set a serious platform is expected to have, (2) **real profit** — actual eBay net earnings (`totalDueSeller`) minus the actual Amazon order cost, which is a comparison of two real transactions rather than a selling price minus an estimated margin, (3) **Setting Groups** — a reusable bundle of pricing rule / stock buffer / HTML template applied to a slice of the catalog, which is the in-app `listing_settings_groups` under a shorter marketing name ("Setting Groups" / "Ayar Grupları"). Value/price is stated in the pricing section and the final CTA as "professional automation without the premium price" — **never as "cheap"**.
  - **Section rhythm was tightened 2026-09-07: `SECTION_Y` 7.5rem → 5.5rem, `SECTION_Y_SM` 4.5rem → 3.5rem.** Three separate reports of dead space above a section heading traced to one structural cause, not to any single section: at 7.5rem two adjacent sections each put 120px of their own padding on either side of the boundary, so every seam carried 240px of empty page and the eyebrow read as detached from its own content. `Section` also gained **`$tightTop`** (halves the top padding to `SECTION_Y_SM`), used only by Features — it follows the pillars, which have bottom padding of their own, and the background change plus border between them already separates the two. `Pillars` likewise gained bottom padding (`spacing.xxl`): the next element is a `Section $alt`, whose colour edge otherwise landed directly under the last line of pillar text, and the section's own top padding sits BELOW that edge so it cannot fill the gap above it.
  - **`S.FlowStrip` carries a real `padding-top` (`SECTION_Y_SM`, `spacing.xxl` ≤900px), and it is load-bearing.** `Hero` ends on a hard dark-navy → light edge (there is deliberately no gradient — see the Hero notes), so with `padding-top: 0` the "the full automation flow" label sat directly against that edge and read as part of the navy band rather than as the first thing on the light page.
  - **`S.FlowStrip` names the whole pipeline, not a three-chip sandwich.** It was `supplier → SellerHill → your eBay store`, which said we sit in the middle but not what we do there. It is now eight chips from `FLOW_STEPS` (ASIN → eBay listing → AI title → price & stock sync → automatic order → tracking update → buyer messages → real profit), wrapping to two rows, with the accent on the last chip so the differentiator reads as the end of the automation rather than a separate product.
  - **The `#profit` panel shows the arithmetic, then the tiers.** `PROFIT_CALC_ROWS` renders the worked sum (eBay sale → fees → net earnings → Amazon order cost → real profit) above `landing.profit.panel.formula`, and only then the confirmed / estimated / unknown tiers mirroring `cost_capture_status`. Figures in the ladder are illustrative constants in the component, like the tier totals beside them. The tier copy is deliberately plainer than it was — "Nothing invented" / "a missing cost stays a dash" was philosophy where a seller needed "know which numbers are final".
  - **Claims are bounded by what actually ships, and two are easy to overstate.** Price/stock sync is *scheduled* — `KEEPA_REFRESH_INTERVAL_MINUTES` defaults to 720, so the copy says "about twice a day" and the FAQ says explicitly that it is not real-time. Tracking conversion is opt-in with a carrier scope (`store_settings.tracking_conversion_provider` defaults to `local`, `tracking_conversion_scope` to `amazon_logistics_only`), so the copy says "when conversion is enabled", never that every number is converted. **There is no product research / discovery / sourcing feature anywhere in the product** — the workflow starts from ASINs the seller supplies, and copy must never imply otherwise.
- **No fabricated social proof, and no architecture in the copy.** The previous page claimed "2,500+ dropshippers / 50K+ orders / 99.9% uptime" and carried three invented named testimonials — all removed (fabricated endorsements are an FTC and TR advertising-law exposure, quite apart from being unbelievable pre-launch). The replacement is benefit copy only. **Internal mechanism is deliberately never named** — no Keepa, no LLM, no aspect ladder, no proxy pool, no queue names, no column names: sellers don't care and competitors read landing pages. Describe the outcome ("item details buyers filter by are filled in for you"), never the machinery.
- Copy is in i18n under `landing.*` (both `en/translation.json` and `tr/translation.json`); the two locales are kept structurally identical — 200 leaf keys each, and the 2026-09-06 rewrite was applied by a script that asserts that parity before writing, and refuses to write unless `JSON.parse` → `JSON.stringify(…, 2)` + CRLF round-trips byte-identically first (these files are CRLF, so a naive rewrite reformats all ~760 lines and buries the real diff). **Turkish is written natively, not translated from the English** — the two say the same thing and are not sentence-for-sentence parallel.
  - Section keys renamed by that rewrite: `landing.perProduct.*` → `landing.settingGroups.*` (`productA/B/C` → `groupA/B/C`, labels `margin`/`stock`/`template` → `pricing`/`stock`/`template`), and `landing.profit.honest` → `landing.profit.actuals`. `landing.flow.source|engine|destination` became `landing.flow.steps.*`. `howItWorks` went 3 → 5 steps and `faq` 6 → 12 questions (`FAQ_KEYS`/`STEPS` in the component are the source of truth for how many render). Dead keys removed at the same time: `profit.tabs.pnlRows`, `ctaBanner.variant1`, `footer.links.faq`, `footer.companyLinks.about|blog|careers`, `pricing.unlimited|listingsLabel|ordersLabel`.
  - **`S.Steps` uses `minmax(12.5rem, 1fr)`, not the old 16rem** — at 16rem the `auto-fit` grid fits four across and orphans the fifth step on a row of its own.
  - **The TR `catalogFallback` numbers use `en-US` grouping ("2,000"), which looks wrong for Turkish and is deliberate**: the live path formats limits with a hardcoded `new Intl.NumberFormat('en-US')` in `LandingPage.container.tsx`, so a locale-correct "2.000" in the fallback would render differently from the same figure on the live path. Fix the container first if this should be locale-aware; do not "correct" the fallback alone. **Plan names and descriptions are NOT landing copy — they come from the `billing` namespace** (`t('billing:billing.plans.<slug>.name|description')`), the same keys `/billing` renders, so the twelve-tier catalog has exactly one set of labels and the two surfaces cannot drift. `landing.pricing.*` holds only what is landing-specific: the shared `included.*` feature list, the `upToListings`/`upToOrders` quota templates, `planCta`, and the `showAllPlans`/`showFewerPlans` expander labels. Live prices come from the public billing catalog; the i18n `catalogFallback` (`nano`/`starter`/`growth`/`pro`) is used only when that call fails and **must stay aligned with the real catalog** — it previously advertised a free 25-listing plan and a $149 "Enterprise" tier that do not exist, and later a $39/$55/$75 ladder that migration `083` replaced. It now carries only `price`/`listings`/`orders`; its names and descriptions come from `billing` like the live path's.
  - **Feature bullets are one shared list, not a per-plan ladder.** The catalog meters exactly two things (active listings, monthly automatic orders) and gates no feature behind a tier, so every card shows its own two quota lines plus the same `included.*` bullets. The old per-slug `features` arrays with "Everything in Starter" framing were inventing a feature ladder the product does not enforce — and would have needed twelve copies per locale.
- **The canonical SellerHill logo is `apps/web/public/logo.svg` (delivered 2026-08-24, cropped+relocated same day).** `packages/ui/src/atoms/Logo/` centrally renders it, so the landing (`NavBrand`, `MobileMenuHead`, `FooterBrand` — all pinned to `layout="full"`) and every auth screen use exactly this asset. Do not recreate the mark as text, duplicate its raw markup in TypeScript, or move/rename the file. `height` remains the preferred sizing prop (`size` is the deprecated fallback); the image preserves its ~4.72:1 ratio and shrinks within constrained containers. Desktop collapsed sidebars hide `LogoArea`; there is no separate icon-only logo.
  - **`layout="wordmark"` renders a second asset, `apps/web/public/logo-wordmark.svg` (icon-less lettermark, added 2026-09-02).** It is the SAME 10 letter paths lifted verbatim from `logo.svg` (6 white "SELLER" + 4 amber "HILL") with the mountain-badge path removed and the `viewBox` refitted to the letters' bounding box (`1471.94 2320.63 2907.35 358.73`, ≈8.1:1) — no font, byte-identical letterforms, so it is not "recreating the mark as text". The expanded **seller + operator sidebars** (`AppLayout`/`OperatorLayout` `S.LogoArea`, which already passed `layout="wordmark"`) use it — that header sits above the nav on the dark `colors.sidebar.background`, and dropping the badge makes the text legible at the 30px slot height. `layout="default"`/`"full"` still render the full badge+wordmark `logo.svg`. **Every surface `wordmark` renders on must be dark** (white "SELLER" is hardcoded) — this is why the landing keeps `layout="full"`: its `MobileMenu` panel is the near-white `colors.landing.heroBg`. To regenerate the asset after a `logo.svg` change, re-measure the letter bbox with a throwaway headless `getBBox` and refit the `viewBox` + the `WORDMARK_WIDTH`/`WORDMARK_HEIGHT` constants in `Logo.component.tsx`.
  - **Provenance**: the delivered file was `Teslim(2026-08-24)/Svg.svg` — a vector mark (mountain glyph + "SELLER" in white + "HILL" in amber) sitting inside a mostly-empty `viewBox="0 0 5000 5000"`. Its `viewBox` was tightened to the artwork's real bounding box (`525.23 2081.57 3949.53 836.86`, ~8% vertical / ~3% horizontal padding) so it renders edge-to-edge instead of at ~15% scale inside a huge empty square; nothing else in the file was touched. The sibling `Svg(Efektli).svg` (an embedded-raster/effects variant used briefly beforehand) and the rest of the delivered `Teslim(2026-08-24)/` folder were deleted once `logo.svg` was extracted — do not recreate that folder or reference either deleted path.
  - **The asset carries its own colours — "SELLER" is pure white.** It no longer inherits `currentColor`, uses Montserrat-generated text, splits `seller`/`hill` into styled runs, or depends on `HillAccent`. Because "SELLER" is hardcoded white, **every surface the logo renders on must be dark** — this is why the landing footer (`LandingPage.style.ts` `Footer`) was switched from the light `colors.landing.sectionDeep` to `colors.sidebar.background` (with `colors.sidebar.divider` for its border/divider), and `FooterDescription`/`FooterColTitle`/`FooterLink`/`Copyright` were repointed from `landing.hero*`/`text.tertiary` tokens to `sidebar.text`/`sidebar.textMuted` — the footer's own `LanguageTrigger`/`LanguageText` call site also gained the `$onDark` prop for the same reason. Container text colour such as `NavBrand`'s `colors.sidebar.text` affects surrounding controls only, not the logo image itself.
  - **Landing sizes remain intentionally different by context:** navbar `height={38}`, mobile-menu head `height={32}`, footer `height={34}`. Auth pages pass a large preferred height but their wrappers constrain the wide artwork to the available panel width with proportional `img` sizing.
  - **Navbar right cluster restyled 2026-08-13 to match sellerboard's plain, uncluttered treatment**: the language `Dropdown` no longer sits inside a `UtilityGroup` wrapper (removed — it only ever held the dropdown plus the now-deleted `ThemeToggle`); `LoginButton`'s `$onDark` (navbar) variant lost its border and gained a `user` icon, rendering as a plain icon+text link like sellerboard's "Giriş yap" — the `$block` (mobile-menu) variant is untouched and keeps its bordered-button look, since there it's one of two stacked CTAs, not a lightweight nav item. `NavCta` ("Kaydolun"/"Ücretsiz başla") dropped its trailing arrow icon to match sellerboard's plain button label. `NavActions`' gap widened (`spacing.sm` → `spacing.md`) to give the decluttered group room to breathe.
- **Icon names are whatever's actually registered in `packages/ui/src/atoms/Icon/icons/index.tsx` — verify before using one, don't assume from prose.** An earlier revision of this file claimed `amazon`/`ebay` brand icons existed in the map; they do not (confirmed by grep, 2026-08-13). Marketing icons available: `arrow-right`, `star`, `zap`/`sparkles`, `shield-check`, `clock`, `layers`, `repeat`, `gauge`, `rocket`, `storefront`, `chart-line`, `bolt`, `eye`. Icon `color` resolves `"brand.primary"` / `"semantic.success"` / `"landing.<token>"` dot-paths.
- **Structurally rebuilt against sellerboard.com as a reference (2026-08-13, hero pass same day) — content stays ours, fabricated-proof sections stay out.** sellerboard's page leans on press logos, a 46-entry testimonial carousel, a "20K+ sellers" count, an agency program and multi-user roles — none of which SellerHill can show honestly pre-launch, so none were copied; the anti-fabrication rule above still governs. What *was* adopted structurally:
  - **Hero is sellerboard's dark-navy split layout, not a centered light block.** `S.HeroInner` is a two-column grid (`minmax(0,1fr) minmax(0,1.05fr)`, collapsing to one column ≤980px), left-aligned, on the dark `S.Hero` fill described above. Its top padding must clear the fixed navbar (~82px) and then leave a deliberate gap: 9.5rem left ~70px of empty navy above the eyebrow and read as a gap rather than breathing room, so it is 7.5rem (~38px) / 6.5rem below 980px. Left column: **eyebrow** → headline → subheading → CTA row → a trial note block. The eyebrow (`S.HeroEyebrow`, `landing.hero.eyebrow` = "Amazon → eBay automation") states the category above the headline so a visitor who already knows the competitors places the product in the first second; it needs its own styled component because the light-surface `S.Eyebrow` uses `landing.chipBg` + `brand.primary`, which all but disappears on the navy hero — `HeroEyebrow` uses `sidebar.hover` / `sidebar.divider` / `landing.accentAmber`. Trial note block is (`S.HeroNote`/`S.HeroNoteTitle`/`S.HeroNoteLine` — bold title line + two muted detail lines, `landing.hero.trialTitle`/`trialNoCard`/`trialCancelAnytime`, mirroring sellerboard's own note under its primary CTA). Right column: the real dashboard screenshot in the shared `PreviewFrame` mount, with `S.HeroFloatCard` — a real cropped KPI card, not fabricated numbers — absolutely positioned overlapping its top-right corner, mirroring sellerboard's floating "Month to date" card. `HeroFloatCard` is hidden ≤980px to avoid mobile overlap; the hand-drawn KPI/chart/table mockup that used to fill this space (`PreviewBody`/`PreviewRail`/`PreviewKpi*`/`PreviewChart*`/`PreviewTable*`/`PreviewRow*`, plus the `landing.heroPreview.*` i18n block and `landing.hero.badge`) is deleted — a real screenshot replaced it outright, so keeping the mockup's styles/copy around would just be dead weight.
  - **Two things that used to sit in the left column are gone (2026-08-13): the "tools" checklist and the eBay/Amazon "works with" chips.** The checklist (`HeroToolsLabel`/`HeroToolsGrid`/`HeroToolItem`/`HeroToolIcon`, `landing.hero.toolsLabel`) briefly reused the `FEATURES` titles/icons, then was deleted outright on request — the full Features section further down the page still shows all six, untouched. The chips (`HeroIntegrations`/`HeroIntegrationsLabel`/`HeroIntegrationChips`/`HeroIntegrationChip`, `landing.hero.integrationsLabel`) were replaced by the trial-note block above — sellerboard's own hero doesn't carry a "works with" row either.
  - **Navbar mega-menu-lite**: "Özellikler/Features" is a `Dropdown` (not a plain scroll link) listing all six automation features with icons; picking one sets the active features-tab AND scrolls to `#features`, so the menu and the section it opens always agree.
  - **Features section is a tabbed deep-dive, not a static 6-card grid**: `S.FeatureTabsLayout` — a vertical tab list on the left (icon + title), a detail panel on the right (icon + title + a longer `.detail` paragraph per feature, new i18n key) plus a real screenshot in the shared matted frame (`S.PreviewFrame`/`S.PreviewImage`). State (`activeFeatureKey`) lives in the component, same as the existing FAQ/reveal state — landing is exempt from the container/component split.
  - **Profit section gained the same tab treatment**: a `TabNav` (`variant="pill"`) switches between the original three-tier `ProfitPanel` ("Overview") and two real screenshots ("P&L" → dashboard P&L tab, "Per order" → orders list) in the same matted frame.
  - **Every screenshot sits in a matted frame, NOT a fake browser window (2026-09-06).** `S.PreviewFrame` used to draw a title bar with red/amber/green traffic lights and an `app.sellerhill.com/dashboard` URL (`PreviewBar`/`PreviewDot`/`PreviewUrl`, all three deleted). That chrome was decoration posing as evidence — it drew attention to a browser we never captured, cost ~40px of vertical space per screenshot for a URL nobody reads, and dated the page the way skeuomorphic mockups do. The frame is now a thin `surface.primary` mount (`spacing.xs+` padding, `radius.2xl`, `landing.cardBorder` hairline, `shadowStrong`) with `PreviewImage` one radius tier below it (`radius.xl` + a `border.secondary` hairline, so a near-white screenshot edge cannot bleed into the mount). Same separation from the page behind it, none of the costume.
  - **Every feature tab shows the screen that actually does the thing it claims (2026-09-06).** It used to be four images across six tabs — `orders.jpg` answered "automatic orders", "tracking updates" AND "automated buyer messages", and "multiple eBay stores" showed the dashboard. A visitor who clicks "buyer messages" and gets an order list reads the whole set as stock photography, which defeats the point of capturing real UI at all. Current pairing: `asinListing`→`listings`, `priceStock`→`listing-detail` (stock vs. Amazon stock, margin, automation status), `autoOrder`→`orders`, `tracking`→`order-detail`, `buyerMessages`→`buyer-messages` (the Message Templates drawer, all four events), `multiStore`→`stores` (the Connected Stores drawer, two stores). The profit section's **"Per order" tab shows `order-detail`, not the order list**: that screen renders the section's own formula as real UI (order earnings − total Amazon cost = net profit). `dashboard-cards.jpg` was deleted — nothing referenced it once `multiStore` moved to `stores.jpg`, and it was a byte-identical copy of `hero-dashboard.jpg`.
  - **`S.FeaturePreviewFrame` caps the tab screenshot at `21rem` (`15rem` ≤900px).** At natural height a 1600×1120 capture renders ~600px tall there, which pushed the image below the fold — clicking a tab appeared to do nothing until you scrolled — and gave every tab a different panel height, so the section jumped on each click. The cap makes the panel a stable height, so content swaps *in place* beside the tab list. It crops with `object-fit: cover; object-position: top`, keeping each screen's identifying top (title, filters, first rows) rather than letterboxing it into an unreadable strip. The rule is `img { … }`, a plain element selector — **never** an Emotion component selector, which needs the babel plugin this app does not run and crashes at runtime.
  - **A newly added file under `public/` is invisible to an already-running Vite dev server** — it answers the SPA fallback (`text/html`, ~1.2KB) with a 200, so `<img>` fails with `naturalWidth === 0` while `curl`/`fetch` HEAD looks fine and the file on disk is a valid JPEG. It is a dev-server-only artifact (`vite build` copies `publicDir` wholesale), so it is not a code defect — but it will make a correct change look broken. Restart the dev server, or verify on a second one (`npx vite --port 5199`), before concluding anything about new screenshots.
  - **Screenshots are captured ONCE PER LOCALE (2026-09-07)**: `apps/web/public/landing-screens/{en,tr}/{dashboard-pnl,orders,order-detail,listings,listing-detail,buyer-messages,stores,hero-dashboard,hero-kpi-card}.jpg` — the same nine file names in both folders, so only the folder varies. The app inside the screenshot IS the product: a Turkish visitor reading Turkish copy over an English dashboard is being shown a different product than the one they would get. `screenSrc(key, locale)` in the component resolves the folder (`tr` prefix → `tr/`, anything else → `en/`), and `FEATURES` carries a `screen` key rather than a baked path. **Adding a screenshot means capturing it twice** — a file present in one folder only 404s in the other locale. All captured from the sign-up-free demo account (`sessionStorage.sellerhill_demo=1` + navigate, see "Sign-up-free demo" above). `hero-kpi-card.jpg` is an **element** screenshot (`page.locator('[role="button"][aria-pressed="true"]')` — the selected `PeriodCard`; the unqualified `[aria-pressed]` matches all four cards and fails Playwright's strict mode), not a manual crop, so it is pixel-real UI, not a redraw. JPEG at quality ~90 to keep each under ~200KB.
  - **Recapture recipe (2026-09-06, the current set was taken this way).** Locale **`en`** — `localStorage.locale='en'` before navigating, since the app otherwise follows the browser's language and the previous set shipped a Turkish UI on the English page. Viewport **1600×1120**: wide enough that the app renders its desktop layout, and tall enough that each page's content ends near the bottom edge — the earlier set was captured tall and left the lower third empty white, which reads as a half-finished product when scaled down. Measure before choosing a height (`max(getBoundingClientRect().bottom)` over `main *`) rather than guessing. **Hide the demo banner before shooting** (`DemoBanner`'s fixed bar, "Sample data — nothing is saved / Exit demo / Create free account") plus scrollbars, by injecting a throwaway `<style>` — it is real and correct in the demo, but on a marketing page it advertises that the screenshot is not a real store. The six feature tabs reuse the four non-hero images (`listings.jpg` ×2, `orders.jpg` ×3, `dashboard-cards.jpg` ×1) rather than one screenshot per claim. **If the UI changes, these go stale silently** (nothing re-derives them) — recapture via a throwaway Playwright script when the dashboard/orders/listings screens change materially.
  - **The monthly/annual toggle is gone (2026-08-21), along with every annual field.** It briefly existed (`SegmentedControl`, mirroring sellerboard's) and read genuine `BillingInterval.ANNUAL` catalog prices. Migration `083` removed annual prices outright — the monthly figures were set at what the annual rate would have been — so the toggle's "Annual" tab would have shown the monthly price under an annual label. `LandingPricingPlan` now carries `priceDisplayMonthly` only; `priceDisplayAnnual`, `priceDisplayAnnualPerMonth`, `annualSavingsMonths`, the `LandingBillingInterval` type and the `PricingToggleRow`/`PricingSavingsBadge`/`PlanPeriodNote` styles are all deleted. Do not reintroduce the toggle without first adding real annual rows to `billing_plan_prices`.
  - **Twelve tiers, four shown: the grid expands in place.** `LandingPricingPlan.isFeatured` marks the four one-per-band plans (`nano`/`starter`/`growth`/`pro`, `growth` badged "most popular"); a `showAllPlans` control under the grid reveals the remaining eight. It is an in-page expander rather than a link because **`/billing` sits behind `AppLayout` auth** — a visitor following a "see all plans" link would land on the login screen, so the page has to be able to show every tier itself. If no featured slug matches the catalog (a rename), the collapsed view falls back to the whole catalog rather than rendering an empty section.
  - **Footer's "Company" column dropped the About/Blog/Careers buttons**, which had no `onClick` and went nowhere (dead links). It now has exactly one entry, "Contact", wired to `window.Tawk_API?.maximize?.()` — opens the same tawk.to widget already live on the page, no new surface.
  - **Not adopted**: sellerboard's marketplace-coverage map (SellerHill is eBay-US + Amazon-US only today; a map would overclaim), the multi-user "define roles" section (no such customer-facing feature exists), the agencies section (no agency program exists).
  - **`PrimaryButton` gained a `$accent` prop** (amber fill / navy text, 2026-08-13 — renamed from an earlier `$invert` white-fill/blue-text variant) for the Hero's primary CTA only — the default blue-fill/white-text reads fine on every light section but would barely separate from the dark-navy hero. `$accent` uses `colors.landing.accentAmber` (the same amber `HillAccent` sets "hill" in the wordmark) for the fill and `colors.sidebar.background` (navy) for the text, matching sellerboard's own orange primary CTA and tying the button to the logo's own brand color. Every other `PrimaryButton` call site (Demo band, etc.) omits it and stays the default.
  - **Navbar links (`NavLink`, `NavDropdownTrigger`) render at full `colors.sidebar.text`, not the muted variant (2026-08-13)** — matching sellerboard.com/tr's own nav item brightness. Weight is unchanged; hover now only adds the background tint since there's no dimmer resting state to brighten out of.
  - **`HeroPreview` carries `margin-top: -2.75rem` (2026-08-13)** to sit higher against the headline — `HeroInner`'s grid centers the preview column against the taller left content column by default, which put the screenshot lower than sellerboard's own reference.
  - **The Hero's short "tools" teaser checklist is deleted outright (2026-08-13)** — `HeroToolsLabel`/`HeroToolsGrid`/`HeroToolItem`/`HeroToolIcon` and the `landing.hero.toolsLabel` i18n key are all removed (it briefly reused `FEATURES` minus `multiStore`, then was cut entirely on the next request). The full Features section further down the page is untouched and still shows all six.
  - **Hero secondary CTA copy is "Demo hesap" / "Demo account" (2026-08-13, was "Canlı demoyu aç" / "Open live demo")** — `landing.hero.ctaSecondary`, matching sellerboard's own "Demo hesap" label next to the play-arrow icon.
  - **Typography is sellerboard's own, not the app's Inter/Lexend (2026-08-13).** Landing is the one surface exempt from the app's type system, and after the hero/font pass it now actually uses that exemption: `LandingPage.style.ts` declares its own `FONT_HEADING` (`'Montserrat', Verdana, -apple-system, …`) and `FONT_BODY` (`'Poppins', Verdana, -apple-system, …`) constants plus a literal `TYPE` size scale (`h1/h2/h3/cardLg/cardMd/cardSm/price/lead/body/small/micro`), and both families are added to the shared Google Fonts `<link>` in `apps/web/index.html` (`Montserrat:wght@600;700;800` + `Poppins:wght@400;500;600;700`) alongside the app's own Inter/Lexend/JetBrains Mono — one request, still cached across the whole site, but only the landing page's CSS ever references the two new families so the extra `@font-face` rules cost nothing elsewhere. **These two families stay landing-only** — putting them on the app was tried on 2026-09-03 and rejected on sight; see the Font history note under "Figma Redesign".
    - **Values are copied from sellerboard.com/tr's live computed styles, not invented or reused from `typographyTokens`** — read via a throwaway Playwright script (`getComputedStyle` on `h1`/`h2`/`p`/nav/buttons), not guessed from the page source: h1 56px/700/1.14, h2 48px/700/1.17, h3-tier ("Doğru kâr panosuyla tanışın", the final-CTA banner) 40px/700, lead paragraphs (hero/section subtitles) 20px/400/1.6, nav links 14px, general body copy 16px, small print ~13px, the pricing figure 32px/700 **in Poppins, not Montserrat** (sellerboard's own price display is Poppins-bold, confirmed live — copying the h1 face onto a number would be a guess, not a match).
    - **Only true page/section-level headlines are Montserrat**: `HeroTitle` (h1), `SectionTitle` (h2, one per section), `SplitTitle` (Profit + Demo band), `CtaTitle` (final banner). Every card/component-level title (`FeatureDetailTitle`, `PillarTitle`, `StepTitle`, `ProfitItemTitle`, `PlanName`, `PlanAmount`, `FooterColTitle`, …) is Poppins — sellerboard's own smaller headings (modal titles, card headers, the price figure) are Poppins-bold too, not Montserrat; treating every `<h3>` as a Montserrat headline would have been truer to our old app-wide "heading font" habit than to sellerboard's actual page.
    - **`font-weight: 700` is a literal on every element this pass touched, not `tkn('typography.fontWeight.bold')`.** That token was softened 700→600 in the app's own 2026-08 type pass (see "Figma Redesign" below) — reusing it here would have silently drawn sellerboard's "bold" one weight lighter than the reference. Landing already hardcodes its own literal sizes for the same reason; weight follows the same rule for consistency.
    - **If sellerboard changes its fonts or sizes, this drifts silently** — nothing re-derives these values. Re-run the same live-computed-style probe against `sellerboard.com/tr` (or whatever locale) rather than eyeballing a screenshot.

### Sign-up-free demo (`apps/web/src/features/demo/`)

Sellerboard converts on a "Kayıt gerekmez!" demo link that drops a visitor straight into a seeded dashboard. SellerHill does the same thing **from browser fixtures instead of a seeded server account** — no shared mutable account whose edits leak between visitors, no extra auth surface to guard, and no eBay/Keepa/LLM quota spent on someone who is only looking. Those quotas are metered per application and shared by every real seller, which is the deciding argument.

- **The flag is read ONCE at module load** (`demoMode.ts`, `sessionStorage` key `sellerhill_demo`), and entering/leaving is a **full document navigation, never a router push**. That is what stops the Redux store and the RTK Query cache from ending up holding a mix of real and sample responses. `sessionStorage` also scopes it per tab, so the demo cannot disturb a real session in another tab.
- `baseApi`'s `baseQueryWithReauth` short-circuits to `demoBaseQuery` before the network when the flag is set. `AuthBootstrap` needs no demo branch: `POST /auth/refresh` is answered with a sample user, so the app comes up "already signed in" through the normal path.
- **A write resolves as a benign success and persists nothing — it must NEVER reject.** Returning a 403 was the obvious first design and it was wrong: feature containers `.catch()` their mutations and raise their own generic red "an error occurred" modal, so clicking **Upgrade** on the billing page greeted the visitor with a failure dialog. `demoWrite` instead answers with a payload that carries no side effect, so the container's success path runs and does nothing; the tag invalidation refetches the same fixtures and the screen stays put. Two GETs need the same treatment because they hand out live external surfaces: `/billing/portal` and `/ebay/connect-url` answer with an **empty URL**, which every caller guards on (`if (result.portalUrl) …`). `/billing/checkout` returns `checkoutUrl: null` for the same reason — a real URL there would send a visitor to a payment page.
- Every write broadcasts one `DEMO_READONLY_EVENT`; `DemoBanner` listens and shows a single friendly **info** modal ("this is a demo, changes are not saved"), so no mutation site in the app has to know demo mode exists.
- **Unmapped GETs answer empty rather than erroring**, so a screen the fixtures do not populate renders its own empty state instead of a red banner that reads as a broken product.
- **No provider, no third party, nothing external — verified, not assumed.** Zero requests reach the API in demo mode, and Keepa / eBay / Amazon / the LLM are all backend concerns, so a backend that is never called cannot invoke them. The one external script the shell loads is **tawk.to**, and `TawkToWidget` now returns `null` and injects nothing when `isDemoMode()` — a demo visitor is not a customer with a support case, and the embed would put them in a real agent queue. The only outbound requests left are Google Fonts from `index.html`, which serve the whole site including the landing page.
- **Coverage is the whole shell, not just the reporting screens.** One fixture file (`demoData.ts`) holds both what the business *did* — listings, orders, dashboard, action centre — and how it is *set up*: profile, two eBay stores, two Amazon buyer accounts, global + per-store settings with a blacklist, buyer-message templates, three listing strategy groups, business policies, listing-job history, and an active Growth subscription mid-period. Every route in the sidebar renders real content; a demo where half the navigation lands on "nothing here yet" reads as an unfinished product.
- Fixtures (`demoData.ts`) are generated from a fixed seed and anchored to "now". Dashboard period totals are **aggregated from the sample order rows**, not typed in, so the cards can never contradict the order list behind them — and the confirmed/estimated/unknown split shown on the landing is the same split the demo dashboard computes. The first three orders are pinned to the last few hours and fully cost-captured, so the "Today" card never greets a visitor with $0.
- Files: `demoMode.ts`, `demoData.ts` (**one** fixture file — do not split it again), `demoBaseQuery.ts`, `DemoBanner/` (4-file split), mounted once as `<DemoBanner />` in `App.tsx` (renders `null` outside demo mode). i18n under `demo.*`.

### Legal documents (`apps/web/src/features/legal/`, 2026-09-07)

Privacy Policy at `/:locale/privacy` and Terms of Service at `/:locale/terms`, both public, EN + TR, plus locale-less `/privacy` and `/terms` redirects. Linked from the landing footer (the Legal column's three buttons had no `onClick` and went nowhere; "Cookies" now points at `/privacy#cookies` rather than a separate cookie policy, because section 13 of the privacy policy IS the cookie disclosure).

- **THE TEXT IS THE OPERATOR'S, NOT OURS.** These documents are supplied verbatim and carry legal liability. Do not translate a missing section, do not fill a gap from the other locale, do not delete a clause that contradicts the code, do not rewrite wording for precision. When a discrepancy is found — and several were, see below — **report it and let the operator decide**. Editing a legal document unilaterally is invisible in a wall of text and destroys the operator's ability to trust that what shipped is what they approved.
- **Content is STRUCTURED BLOCKS in the i18n `legal` namespace**, not prose or raw HTML. `LegalBlockType` (`packages/shared/src/domain/legal/`) is `p | h | ul | ol | dl | address | email`. Raw HTML in a translation file cannot be style-tokenised and would be the one surface in the app with hardcoded colours; one-key-per-paragraph loses the list structure these documents lean on, and a legal list rendered as a run-on sentence changes what the document appears to say.
- **`parseLegalDocument` narrows the i18next `returnObjects` payload and fails soft** — a malformed block is dropped, a malformed section is dropped, a document with no usable sections resolves to `null` and the page shows `legal.unavailable`. A legal page that throws is strictly worse than one that renders a little less. This is also what lets the renderer's switch stay total over `LegalBlockType` with no guessing default.
- **Parity is asserted at the SECTION level only, never block-for-block.** The Turkish is written natively, and Turkish grammar trails a list with its governing verb (`SellerHill: <list> … değildir.`), so it legitimately needs three blocks where English needs two. Both documents carry the same section ids in the same order; block counts differ and that is correct.
- **The URL locale outranks the stored preference, and the container has to enforce it.** `i18n.config.ts` initialises i18next from `localStorage`/browser only, so a visitor whose stored preference is Turkish opening a shared `/en/privacy` link was served the Turkish document under an English URL (observed live). `LegalDocumentPageContainer` syncs `i18n.changeLanguage` to the path segment — deliberately WITHOUT writing the preference, since following one link is not a decision to switch the whole app over.
- **`Text` always sets its own colour and never inherits**, so a label on the dark header must name a `sidebar.*` token explicitly (`color="sidebar.textMuted"`) or it renders near-black on navy. Same class of bug as the landing navbar's `$onDark` props.
- **Headings are `role="heading"` + `aria-level` on wrappers**, because `Text` renders a `<span>` with no element-override prop and the design system forbids raw typography elements. Without it a 51-section legal document exposes no heading structure to a screen reader — and this is exactly the kind of document people navigate by heading.
- `LocaleRedirect` gained **`preserveHash`** so `/privacy#cookies` survives the locale redirect; no other route uses a fragment, so it is off by default.
- **The retention clause was revised to match the code, on operator approval (2026-09-07).** The supplied text promised orders/buyer data was kept "up to 24 months"; `orders` is not in `DATA_RETENTION_RULES` and nothing purges it, so that was never true. It now ties retention to the account lifetime plus the §20 deletion request, which is what actually happens. **If a real purge is ever implemented, this clause has to move back** — it is the one place the document makes a durable promise about a table.
- **Account deletion is a manual email process, by operator decision.** `POST /auth/deactivate` is a soft delete (`status = 'inactive'`) that erases nothing and has no UI entry point; §20 tells the seller to email `privacy@sellerhill.com` and the operator handles it. A self-service delete button would need a real erasure path first.
- **Everything else in both documents was checked against the code and holds**: IP and user-agent really are logged (`logging.interceptor.ts`), the top-up validity period really is displayed before purchase (`billing.addons.note`), audit logs really are 730 days, buyer-message logs 400, fulfillment evidence 7, dormant browser profiles 90. The English Terms §13 used to carry a "no product sourcing or product-research services" disclaimer that the Turkish did not; it was **removed from both** on operator decision (2026-09-08) rather than added to Turkish. It bought almost nothing — §35 (AS IS / no warranties) and §36 (no business results) already cover that ground — and writing the *absence* of a feature into a binding document is a commitment the roadmap should not have to work around. Do not reintroduce a disclaimer for a feature that does not exist yet; the landing-copy rule (never imply product research exists) is about not claiming it, not about denying it in the Terms.

### Custom ESLint Plugin (`eslint-plugin-design-system.js`)

- `no-hardcoded-colors` — Use theme tokens, not hex/rgb
- `no-hardcoded-spacing` — Use theme spacing tokens
- `no-inline-styles` — No style prop in JSX
- `no-styled-typography` — Use `<Text>` component, not styled.h1/h2/etc.
- `no-bare-text-in-button` — Wrap Button children in `<Text>`
- `no-implicit-i18n-namespaces` — Require namespace prefix in i18n keys

## Domain Concepts

- **Product**: Source of truth from Amazon (ASIN, images, price, features). Cached to save API costs. Shared **ASIN + marketplace**-keyed across all users (see "Multi-marketplace architecture" below).
- **Listing**: An active offer on eBay. Linked to a Listing Setting Group **and** an eBay account (`listings.ebay_account_id`, multi-store). Per-listing automation overrides live on the listing row (pause sales, fixed price/qty, margins).
- **Listing Setting Group**: Repricing strategy, stock buffer, fees, HTML templates, plus **content policy** (`content` JSONB: strip brand from title, optional AI title/description flags). Multiple listings share one group.
- **Order**: An eBay sale. Linked to a Listing (`listing_id`) and eBay account (`ebay_account_id`). Product info via listing→product JOIN. Financials on both eBay and Amazon sides.
- **Amazon Account**: Buyer Amazon account for order scraping. AES-256-GCM credentials. Multiple per user. Carries its own `marketplace` (immutable after creation — see "Multi-marketplace architecture").
- **eBay Account**: Connected store(s). Multi-store filtering on listings + orders by `ebayAccountId`. OAuth tokens are **encrypted at rest** (AES-256-GCM via `AMAZON_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, `enc:` prefix in `ebay_accounts.access_token`/`refresh_token`; `EbayService.onModuleInit` lazily re-encrypts legacy plaintext rows). All API callers must obtain tokens via `EbayService.getActiveAccountAccessToken(userId)` / `getAccountAccessToken(accountId)` — never read the raw columns.

## Multi-marketplace architecture (eBay + Amazon, 2026-08-13)

The platform was designed for multi-country from the start but only `eBay.com`/`amazon.com` (US) is live — this section is the seam that lets a second marketplace be enabled later **without a schema migration or a rewrite**, without building actual support for any specific undecided country now.

**One allowlist gates what's selectable, for each integration.** `SUPPORTED_EBAY_MARKETPLACES` / `SUPPORTED_AMAZON_MARKETPLACES` (`packages/shared/src/domain/{ebay,amazon}/*.constants.ts`) are the single place that decides what a picker offers AND what the backend accepts (`EbayService.createConnectUrl` / `AmazonAccountsService.create` both throw `BadRequestException` on a marketplace outside the allowlist). Enabling a second marketplace is one array entry + real config data — never a new code path.

- **eBay was already ~90% there.** `EbayMarketplaceId` (US/UK/DE/FR/IT/ES) and `EBAY_MARKETPLACE_CONFIG` (currency, siteId, countryCode) already existed with verified data for all 6; `ebay_accounts.marketplace_id` was already a real, populated column threaded through `ebay.service.ts`/order-sync/fulfillment, and the OAuth backend already accepted a `marketplaceId` param. The only gap was UI: all 4 eBay-connect call sites (`EbayAccountGuard`, `OnboardingEbayPage`, `StoresPage`, `SettingsHubPage` → `EbayAccountsDrawer`) hardcoded `EBAY_MARKETPLACE.US` with no selector. Each now renders a `ModernSelect` (via `ConnectEbayPrompt`'s new `marketplaceOptions`/`selectedMarketplace`/`onMarketplaceChange` props, or directly in `EbayAccountsDrawer`) sourced from `getEbayMarketplaceOptions(t)` (`apps/web/src/features/ebay/utils/ebayMarketplaceOptions.ts`) — today it always renders exactly one option (US), since `SUPPORTED_EBAY_MARKETPLACES` still holds only `EBAY_US`. `getEbayMarketplaceLabel` is the single label resolver used by the picker, `StoresPage`'s store list and the `EbayAccountCard` mapper, so they can never drift from each other (the eBay `ebay.connect.marketplace{US,UK,DE,FR,IT,ES}` i18n keys were pre-provisioned and unused before this — they're wired in now).
- **Amazon buyer accounts and the Keepa/`products` pipeline were 0% marketplace-aware** — `amazon_accounts` had no marketplace column, `products.asin` was globally unique with no domain qualifier (risky once a second Amazon catalog exists: the same ASIN string can be a different product on a different storefront), `KeepaService` hardcoded Keepa's `domain: 1` (US) and `currency: 'USD'`, and Amazon URLs were scattered `https://www.amazon.com/...` string literals. Closed by:
  - `AmazonMarketplace` enum (`packages/shared/src/domain/amazon/amazon.enums.ts`) — **ships with a single member, `AMAZON_US`**, deliberately not pre-populated like `EbayMarketplaceId`: a second Amazon marketplace's Keepa domain id/TLD are undecided product scope, and fabricating that config now would be building support for a country nobody chose. `AMAZON_MARKETPLACE_CONFIG` (`amazon.constants.ts`) carries `countryCode`/`currency`/`domain` (TLD)/`keepaDomainId` per marketplace, mirroring `EBAY_MARKETPLACE_CONFIG`'s shape.
  - Migration `081`: `amazon_accounts.marketplace VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'AMAZON_US'`. Set **only at create** (`CreateAmazonAccountDto`/`createAmazonAccountSchema`) — **immutable after creation**, enforced structurally by `UpdateAmazonAccountDto` having no matching field, so no code path can even attempt to change it. `AmazonAccountDrawer` renders the marketplace `ModernSelect` in create mode and a locked/disabled one (+ hint text) in edit mode.
  - Migration `082`: `products.marketplace VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'AMAZON_US'`, and the old single-column `products_asin_key` UNIQUE constraint is swapped for a composite `products_asin_marketplace_key UNIQUE (asin, marketplace)` (no FK references `products.asin` — all reference `products.id` — so this was safe). Every query that filters/upserts `products` by `asin` now also takes a `marketplace` parameter defaulting to `AmazonMarketplace.AMAZON_US` (`ListingsService.getProductByAsin`/`findOrCreateProduct` — the latter's `ON CONFLICT` target changed accordingly —, `ListingProcessorService.resolveProductData` including its advisory-lock key, `ProductSyncService`, `ProductsService.getProductPriceAndImageByAsin`/`getProductPriceByAsin`). Since only `AMAZON_US` exists, every default resolves identically to pre-migration behavior.
  - `KeepaService.request()`/`getProducts()`/`getProductDetailsWithMeta()`/`getProductDetails()`/`normalizeProductData()` all take a `marketplace` parameter (default `AMAZON_US`): `request()`'s `domain` param now reads `AMAZON_MARKETPLACE_CONFIG[marketplace].keepaDomainId` instead of the hardcoded `1`, and `normalizeProductData()`'s `currency` reads the config instead of the hardcoded `'USD'`. `RefreshProcessorService.refreshBatch` groups a claimed batch **by marketplace** before calling Keepa (Keepa's `domain` is one value per request, so a batch spanning marketplaces needs one call per group) — at one marketplace this is exactly one group and behavior-identical to the old single-call path.
  - Amazon URL literals centralized behind `buildAmazonSiteUrl(marketplace)` / `buildAmazonProductUrl(asin, marketplace)` (`packages/shared/src/domain/ebay/ebay.urls.ts` — historically the one Amazon-URL home despite the folder name) and threaded through `AmazonCheckoutService` (`checkout()`, `ensureLoggedIn()` — both already had the account row in scope via `loadInputs`, which now also selects `a.marketplace`) and `AmazonScrapingService` (`performLogin`, `doScrapeOrder`, `doScrapeOrderStatus`, `doScrapeAccountOrders`, `ensureAuthenticatedPage`, `testLogin` — all already load the account row via `AmazonAccountsService.getDecrypted`, which now returns `marketplace` for free since it's part of `AmazonAccountRow`).
  - **Deliberately NOT touched** (documented, not silently missed): `amazon-order-parser.service.ts`'s currency-symbol regexes and `keepa-normalizer.ts`'s imperial-unit weight formatting — both are Jest-covered pure modules with zero present-day behavior need (only USD/imperial exists today); `amazon-checkout.service.ts`'s module-scope `CHECKOUT_SELECTORS.cartUrl` and `amazon-scraping.service.ts`'s module-level sign-in URL const (no account context at that scope); `browser-state-manager.service.ts`'s `isSessionValid` (no account/DB dependency in that class today — adding one is disproportionate for a single-marketplace system).
- **Not implemented, deliberately**: no "choose Amazon source marketplace" UI at listing-creation time (there's no user-facing decision point for it today — `resolveProductData` always resolves to `AMAZON_US` via the default parameter), no actual scraping/checkout for a non-US Amazon domain, no currency-symbol-aware order parsing.

## Product Refresh Pipeline

Amazon product data (metadata + Buy Box price + Buy Box stock) is sourced **exclusively from the Keepa API** — one provider, one query shape for every call (create + refresh alike): `history=0&stats=90&offers=20&only-live-offers=1&stock=1`. ScraperAPI was removed: its per-request credit model is 10–100× more expensive than Keepa's token pool under bulk-add/churn. **Full technical reference (verified cost model, response ground truth, invariants, probe procedure): [docs/keepa-integration.md](docs/keepa-integration.md) — read it before touching any Keepa code.**

**Token cost model (verified against live responses, 2026-07-28):** Keepa charges are conditional — `offers` costs **6 tokens per FOUND page of 10 live offers** when Keepa refreshes its offer cache, **0 tokens** when its cache is <1h fresh, and `stock=1` adds **+2 only when the stock observation is fresh**. Observed real costs for the same query ranged 0–6 tokens/ASIN. Therefore: (a) accounting always uses the response-reported `tokensConsumed`, **never** a locally-estimated per-ASIN count; (b) `history=0`/`stats`/`only-live-offers` are payload optimizations, **not** token savers. Real Buy Box stock requires `offers` — the old `stock=1`-without-`offers` call did not return usable per-seller stock.

### Shared product cache
`products` is a shared ASIN-keyed cache — one ASIN exists once regardless of how many users list it. One customer's data fetch serves every customer listing that ASIN. There is **no per-user Keepa cost isolation**; costs are attributed via fair-split (see Token tracking below).

### Stale-driven scheduler + batch worker
There is **no "refresh everything every N hours" cron**. Instead a `keepa-refresh` BullMQ queue with two roles:
- **Scheduler** (`RefreshSchedulerService` registers the tick; disabled entirely when `KEEPA_REFRESH_ENABLED=false`): every tick runs an **atomic claim** — `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` CTE that selects the most-overdue products **having ≥1 ACTIVE listing** and pushes their `next_refresh_at` forward by a short lease (`KEEPA_REFRESH_CLAIM_LEASE_MINUTES`, default 15) in the same statement — then enqueues **one** `refresh-batch` job. Overlapping ticks/parallel workers can never double-claim rows; a crashed batch's rows become due again when the lease expires. Products whose listings are all draft/ended are **not** refreshed (no eBay surface to update — this is a scope rule, NOT per-product sales-velocity tiering, which is explicitly not done).
- **Worker** (`RefreshProcessorService`): one bulk Keepa call per batch (`KeepaService.getProducts` dedupes ASINs and chunks internally at Keepa's 100-ASIN/request limit, summing `tokensConsumed` across chunks) → captures balance → per-product compare + update + fan-out. Per-product failures are isolated inside the batch; a transport failure (429/5xx/network) propagates so BullMQ retries the whole batch idempotently (rows stay claimed during retries).

### Stock semantics (three-state, unknown-preserve)
`KeepaStockStatus` (`known|out_of_stock|unknown` in `packages/shared`): the normalizer (`keepa-normalizer.ts`, pure + fixture-tested) matches the **Buy Box offer by sellerId** in `liveOffersOrder` and reads the last `stockCSV` observation; falls back to `stats.stockBuyBox` / `stats.stockAmazon` (Amazon caps at 1000). `OUT_OF_STOCK` (stock 0) only when live-offer retrieval **succeeded** and no live offer + no price exists. Anything ambiguous → `UNKNOWN`: the worker **preserves the previous price/stock** — Keepa failing to observe is never evidence of $0/qty-0. Keepa price sentinels (`-1` no data, `-2` no Buy Box) are treated as null, never real prices. `totalOfferCount` is **never** used as a stock quantity. `images` array (modern) is preferred over legacy `imagesCSV` (live responses can null the CSV).

### Field change-detection
`commerceChanged` (price/stock) triggers the eBay fan-out (`ProductSyncService.updateAllListingsForProduct` — pushes only where a listing's own price/qty actually changed); `metadataChanged` (title/brand/description) persists to the products row **without** fan-out. eBay API volume stays proportional to real changes.

### Failure handling & poison-product quarantine
- **Transport failure**: BullMQ exponential backoff retries the batch; rows stay claimed (lease), then become due again if all attempts fail.
- **Data failure** (ASIN missing from an otherwise-successful response): `consecutive_failures++` with **escalating backoff 5m → 15m → 60m → 240m** (`refresh-backoff.ts`, tested) so a dead ASIN can't burn tokens every tick; at/above `KEEPA_REFRESH_MAX_FAILURES` it is quarantined (`KEEPA_REFRESH_QUARANTINE_MINUTES`).

### Config-driven frequency (no rewrite to change cadence)
All env (defaults shown), all optional:
- `KEEPA_REFRESH_ENABLED=true` — master switch; `false` removes the repeatable tick (create-path calls unaffected).
- `KEEPA_REFRESH_INTERVAL_MINUTES=720` (12h). Dial to 360/180 by env only.
- `KEEPA_REFRESH_BATCH_SIZE=50` — products claimed per tick (chunked at 100/request internally, so >100 is safe). **This is only the fallback since 2026-08-18** — see the auto-sizing note below.
- `KEEPA_REFRESH_BATCH_AUTO=true` / `KEEPA_REFRESH_RESERVE_PERCENT=20` — **the batch size is derived from the Keepa plan's own refill rate by default.** The scheduler ticks once a minute, so `batchSize` IS the per-minute refresh throughput and has to track the plan: set too high it outruns the refill rate and burns the retry budget on Keepa 429s; set too low the configured refresh interval **stretches silently** (nothing errors — each tick simply cannot take all the products that are already due). Keeping the two matched by hand meant editing a second setting on every plan change, which is exactly the step that gets forgotten. `resolveRefreshBatchSize` (`refresh-batch-size.ts`, pure + Jest-covered) computes `floor(refill_rate × (1 − reserve) / 8 tokens)`, clamped to the registry bounds, from `keepa_balance.refill_rate` — the tpm Keepa itself reports on every response. Upgrading the Keepa plan therefore raises throughput on its own. The reserve mirrors `EBAY_BUDGET_RESERVE_PERCENT`: background refresh never consumes the whole rate, so a seller adding listings (the create path) is not starved. Falls back to the fixed `KEEPA_REFRESH_BATCH_SIZE` when auto is off **or** no balance has been captured yet (fresh install, before the first Keepa call), and the value is always floored rather than rounded up — overshooting the refill rate costs real money in retries, undershooting only slows the cycle.
- `KEEPA_REFRESH_CLAIM_LEASE_MINUTES=15` — claim lease; must exceed worst-case batch runtime incl. BullMQ retries.
- `KEEPA_REFRESH_SCHEDULER_CRON=* * * * *`, `KEEPA_REFRESH_WORKER_CONCURRENCY=1`, `KEEPA_REFRESH_QUARANTINE_MINUTES=1440`, `KEEPA_REFRESH_MAX_FAILURES=5`.
- `KEEPA_UPDATE_HOURS` (unset by default) — Keepa-side cache tolerance: serve Keepa's own cached offer data younger than N hours for ~0 tokens instead of forcing a live scrape (6 tokens/found page). Popular ASINs are refreshed by other Keepa customers, so raising this materially cuts spend; keep well below the refresh interval. `update=-1` (never refresh) is deliberately unsupported — niche ASINs would go permanently stale.

Budgeting: the offers+stock query costs ~6–14 tokens/ASIN on offer-cache refresh (0 when cached). Sustainable throughput ≈ `refill_rate_per_min / avg_tokens_per_asin`; e.g. 20 tpm ÷ 8 ≈ 2.5 ASIN/min ≈ 3.6k ASIN/day. `keepa_balance.refill_rate` (migration `055`) records the plan tier from each response.

### Token tracking (cost attribution)
- `keepa_usage_log` — one row per **requested** ASIN per refresh (fair share = batch `tokensConsumed` / requested-ASIN count — missing ASINs still get their row, so their users are not subsidized by users of returned ASINs), `source ('refresh'|'create')`, `user_ids` (JSONB array of users actively listing the ASIN). Append-only.
- `keepa_balance` — `tokens_left`/`refill_in_ms`/`refill_rate` snapshots captured from **every** Keepa response (refresh + create paths).
- **Admin per-user monthly cost (fair-split):** `SELECT uid, SUM(tokens / jsonb_array_length(user_ids)) AS tokens FROM keepa_usage_log CROSS JOIN LATERAL jsonb_array_elements_text(user_ids) AS uid WHERE requested_at >= date_trunc('month', NOW()) GROUP BY uid ORDER BY tokens DESC;`
- No UI yet (admin reads tables directly). Token-balance-driven admission control is deferred until real utilization data accumulates.

### Listing creation path
`ListingProcessorService` (the `listings` queue worker) resolves product data via `resolveProductData`: cache hit (valid title + ≥1 image) → **0 Keepa tokens**; cache miss → a **pg advisory lock keyed on the ASIN** serializes concurrent creates (recheck-after-lock), so N parallel jobs for one uncached ASIN make exactly **one** `getProductDetailsWithMeta` call. Usage + balance are logged from response meta **before** product validation — tokens charged for empty/invalid responses stay in accounting. New product rows get `next_refresh_at = NOW() + 12h` on insert (`findOrCreateProduct`). On the create path only, `UNKNOWN` stock normalizes to 0 (no previous value exists to preserve; live publish blocks at qty 0, drafts allowed).

### Listing quality: description template, item specifics, identifiers

The published eBay listing is built from three independent pieces. All three had defects that were visible on live listings (raw `{{{product_description}}}` text, a collapsed description box, three item specifics with a literal "Unknown"), so the rules below are load-bearing.

**1. Description — ONE renderer, shared by preview and publish.** `renderListingTemplate` + `buildListingTemplateContext` live in `packages/shared/src/utils/listing-template.ts` and are used by BOTH `ListingStrategyService.processDescriptionTemplate` (what eBay receives) and the settings-drawer live preview. They previously were two implementations with two vocabularies: the preview understood the Mustache syntax every seeded template is written in, the backend understood only `{{title}} {{description}} {{brand}} {{features}}` **and ignored PREDEFINED templates entirely** (`TODO: Handle predefined templates`), so a user's chosen design either never shipped or shipped with unrendered placeholder text.
- Supported syntax: `{{key}}` (escaped; an array renders as `<ul>`), `{{{key}}}` (raw), `{{#key}}…{{.}}…{{/key}}` (section/repeat), `{{^key}}…{{/key}}` (inverted). Canonical keys: `title`, `product_description`, `feature_bullets`, `product_details`, `main_image`, `images`, `brand`, `manufacturer`, `asin`, `category`, `condition`, `price`, `currency`, `quantity`. Legacy aliases (`description`, `features`, `specs`, `image`) still resolve.
- **Three presence flags — `has_features`, `has_details`, `has_images`** (`LISTING_TEMPLATE_PRESENCE_FLAGS`) exist because a section cannot nest inside a section of the **same** key (the closer is matched by backreference), so `{{#product_details}}` cannot both wrap a heading and repeat its own rows. Without them a spec-less product renders a styled heading over a void. They are scalars (`'1'` / `''`, never `'0'` — `isEmptyValue('0')` is false), used only as `{{#has_details}}…{{/has_details}}`, and are deliberately **not** in `LISTING_TEMPLATE_PLACEHOLDERS` (inserting `{{has_features}}` would print a bare `1`).
- `stripUnresolvedPlaceholders` runs last, so **template syntax can never reach a buyer**. Any new placeholder is added in the shared module once — never in one surface only.
- **Three placeholders are silently wrong or empty on a live listing and must never appear in a template**: `condition`/`quantity` are never passed by `processDescriptionTemplate`, and **`price`/`currency` carry the AMAZON SOURCE price** — `processDescriptionTemplate` runs at `listing-strategy.service.ts:61`, *before* `calculatePrice` (line 96), and builds its context from `product.price.current`, so publishing them puts the seller's cost in front of the buyer. `asin` is an Amazon identifier in buyer-visible text. No catalog template uses any of them and `predefined-templates.guard.spec.ts` enforces it; **fixing the price context itself (render the description after pricing) is still open.**
- PREDEFINED templates resolve through `ListingSettingsGroupService.getPredefinedTemplateHtml(id)` (5-min in-process cache); a missing id degrades to `DEFAULT_LISTING_TEMPLATE_HTML`, never to an empty description.

**1a. The template catalog is DATABASE DATA, not code (2026-08-09).** `predefined_templates` used to be a materialized copy of a hardcoded TS array that `ListingSettingsGroupService.onModuleInit` upserted by name on every API boot. That seed, its duplicate self-heal and its group-repointing logic are **deleted**; the catalog now lives in migrations `070` (structure) + `071` (the 8 templates).
- **`slug` is the stable natural key** (migration `070`: derived from `name`, `NOT NULL`, `UNIQUE`). It has to be: `id` is a random UUID referenced from `listing_settings_groups.templates->>'predefinedTemplateId'` **inside JSONB, with no foreign key**, and every deployed database already held rows minted by the old boot seed. A migration that merely INSERTed the catalog would not adopt them — it would create a second copy and detach every user's template choice, which then degrades silently to the default template while the UI keeps showing the chosen name.
