  **A TOTP (authenticator) secret is MANDATORY on every Amazon buyer account (2026-09-06).** Amazon challenges almost every automated/headless sign-in with an OTP, and a TOTP generated from the stored secret is the only challenge the workers can answer without a human — an account with no secret connects and then never leaves `VERIFYING`/`INVALID`. `CreateAmazonAccountDto` / `createAmazonAccountSchema` now require `twoFactorSecret`; `AmazonAccountsService.assertTwoFactorSecret` re-checks presence **and** base32/length via `isValidTotpSecret` (`amazon.errors.twoFactorSecretRequired` / `twoFactorSecretInvalid`). On **edit** it is required only when the row has no stored secret yet (a legacy account, or one already `INVALID` for this reason) — otherwise a blank field keeps the saved one. `AmazonAccountDrawer` validates it on the ACCOUNT step with the `submitAttempted` pattern. No DB constraint / migration — app-level like the `assertCanEnable` cap gate, so existing secret-less rows keep working until their next edit.
  - **`normalizeTotpSecret` / `isValidTotpSecret` moved to `@repo/shared` (`utils/totp-secret.ts`)** — `apps/api/.../amazon/totp-secret.ts` is now a re-export shim. Amazon shows the secret in space-grouped blocks (`ZM7Q ZRGJ …`) and sellers paste it verbatim; the drawer's `twoFactorSecret` input now normalizes on every change with the **same** helper the API uses to store it and feed otplib (strip `\s`/`-`/`_`, uppercase), so the field shows exactly what gets saved and a pasted secret is never rejected for its formatting. `totp-secret.spec.ts` stays in `apps/api` and still covers it through the shim.

  **A failed verification persists a CODE, not the raw provider message (2026-09-06).** `AmazonVerifyProcessorService` runs `classifyAmazonVerificationFailure` (pure, `amazon-verify-helpers.ts`, Jest-covered) over the raw `performLogin` error and writes an `AmazonVerificationFailureCode` (`invalid_credentials | captcha | two_factor_required | auth_not_proven | unexpected_login_page | transport | unknown`) into `amazon_accounts.last_verification_error` — same audience split as `ListingFailureCode` / `AutoFulfillBlockedReason`. The web card localizes it via `settingsHub.sections.amazon.verificationError.<code>` (the mapper collapses any unrecognized value, incl. pre-enum raw strings, to `unknown`). The raw text — which is unbounded and can echo account identifiers / URLs / DOM dumps — stays in the Winston log only. Before this the card rendered `lastVerificationError` verbatim with no `t()`, so it was always English regardless of locale.

**Amazon authentication must be positively PROVEN, never inferred (`amazon-auth-state.ts`).** `probeAmazonAuth` + the pure, unit-tested `decideAmazonAuth` are the single source of truth, used by `performLogin` (post-login gate), `BrowserStateManager.isSessionValid` (session reuse) and `AmazonCheckoutService.ensureLoggedIn` (pre-checkout gate). Precedence: an auth route (`/ap/signin`, `/ax/claim/*`) or a visible credential/OTP control is a HARD negative; auth-gated account content or a populated non-signed-out nav greeting is a positive; `signedOutNav` alone is a WEAK negative that must NOT veto a positive, because Amazon serves its header from a CDN cache that can render "Hello, sign in" on a fully authenticated page. Two bugs made this necessary: (a) `!url.includes('/signin')` treated Unified Auth's `/ax/claim/*` pages as valid sessions, and a submit that merely produced no error was marked ACTIVE — so `AmazonAccountStatus.ACTIVE` was a false positive; (b) `ensureLoggedIn` only probed for captcha/OTP, so a signed-out page silently no-opped every checkout step (each helper treats a missing control as "layout variant, skip") and the run failed only at the review-total read — misreported as `cap`. Hence `review_unreadable` is now a distinct blocked reason from `cap`, and "Proceed to checkout" is a mandatory step (`clickFirstAvailable` returns a boolean; a miss there throws `cart`).
- **`OrderFulfillmentState` is the seller-facing vocabulary** (`packages/shared/src/domain/orders/fulfillment-state.ts`, derived at read time by the pure `deriveFulfillmentState`, unit-tested): `purchased | amazon_cancelled | action_required | in_progress | not_automated | manual | simulated`. It exists because the raw columns could not answer "did Amazon buy this, and must I act?" without knowing the schema — `orders.status` is eBay-only, `auto_fulfill_status` has seven values (several internal), `amazon_cancelled_at` is a separate flag that overrides `placed`, and `cost_capture_status` is a third axis. Precedence is load-bearing: an Amazon cancellation outranks `placed` (money moved, item not coming, eBay sale still owed), and a `SIM-` order id reports SIMULATED so a dry run can never look purchased. The list column, detail page and filter all read this one value; `GET /orders?fulfillmentState=` filters it **in SQL** so paging/totals stay correct. FE mapping (badge variant, icon, per-state guidance key) lives in `features/orders/shared/fulfillment-state.ts`.
- **A settled sale is never action-required (2026-08-12).** `COMPLETED` is the only terminal status the platform writes (`mapOrderStatus` never writes `cancelled`, and an Amazon-side cancellation deliberately leaves `orders.status` alone), so it is the only evidence that the seller resolved things by hand. A completed order therefore reports MANUAL even when `amazon_cancelled_at` is set or auto-fulfill ended `blocked`/`failed`. Without this rule those orders stayed action-required **forever** — there is no acknowledge flag — so the Action Center badge could never reach zero, and an action list that cannot clear stops being read. `SHIPPED` is explicitly NOT settled: an Amazon cancellation after the tracking push means eBay holds a tracking number for a parcel nobody is sending, which is the worst case there is.
- **Fulfillment state has ONE definition, on both sides of the wire.** `buildFulfillmentStateSql(alias)` (`packages/shared/src/domain/orders/fulfillment-state-sql.ts`) emits a single ordered `CASE` mirroring `deriveFulfillmentState` branch for branch; `OrdersService`'s filter and every Action Center order count use it. It replaced **seven independent per-state WHERE predicates that each re-encoded the precedence chain and had already drifted from the function** — the MANUAL predicate matched any non-`placed` order with an `amazon_order_id`, so a BLOCKED order with a linked Amazon id was returned by both the MANUAL and the ACTION_REQUIRED filter while the row itself rendered ACTION_REQUIRED. Seven copies of a precedence chain cannot be kept in step; one ordered chain is the precedence. Only enum constants are interpolated — never caller input.
  - `fulfillment-state-sql.guard.spec.ts` proves equivalence without a database: a small evaluator interprets the generated branch chain and compares it to `deriveFulfillmentState` across **every** combination of status × auto-fulfill status × Amazon id shape × cancellation (288 rows), asserts every state is reachable, and **throws** on any SQL construct it does not recognize — so an unreviewed SQL change cannot pass as green. Edit one side without the other and it fails.
- Orders list shows ONE unified fulfillment column (badge + blocked reason + Amazon order id). The old pairing of a raw `auto_fulfill_status` badge with a separate cancellation badge required schema knowledge to read, and an untouched order rendered a bare em dash. The status filter offers only the statuses the sync actually writes (`pending | waiting_shipment | processing | shipped | completed`) — `mapOrderStatus` never writes `cancelled`, so offering it guaranteed an empty result that read as a broken filter. The former two-value "needs attention" dropdown is replaced by the fulfillment-state filter (`autoFulfillNeedsAttention` is still accepted for API compatibility). Labels i18n'd under `orders.fulfillmentState.*` (EN + TR).
- **A missing Amazon product page is `no_asin`, not `out_of_stock`.** `isProductPageMissing` checks HTTP 404/410 and Amazon's own "couldn't find that page" screen before the availability check. Reporting a delisted ASIN as out-of-stock told the seller to wait for stock that will never return; the two need different actions (fix/end the listing vs. wait).
- **Shared enums**: `AutoFulfillStatus` (`pending|running|placed|blocked|failed|dry_run|skipped`) + `AutoFulfillBlockedReason` (13 values incl. `proxy_required`, `quota_exhausted`, `cart`, `review_unreadable`) in `packages/shared/src/domain/orders/orders.types.ts`; `TrackingConversionProvider` (`local|api`) in `packages/shared/src/domain/amazon/amazon.types.ts`. The `auto-fulfill-helpers.ts` re-exports a template-literal type derived from the enum (single source of truth).

### Tracking conversion (Aquiline) — what the eBay buyer sees

Decides the tracking number pushed to eBay at the shipped transition. **Used by every shipped order** (auto-fulfill, manual link, auto cost-capture alike), resolved per-order from `store_settings.tracking_conversion_provider` with the usual Store > Global > Default precedence.

**Two providers.** `LOCAL` (default) passes the Amazon number through under its real carrier (`Amazon_Logistics`, or the mapped `UPS`/`USPS`/`FedEx`/`DHL_Express`) — honest, but it tells the buyer who the supplier is. `AQUILINE` converts it to an `AQUAA…YQ` number carried under the `AQUILINE` carrier.

**Which carriers get converted is the SELLER's setting, never an implicit rule in the service (2026-08-21).** `TrackingConversionService` used to convert every carrier unconditionally, and its own comment explained both why a hardcoded `TB[A-Z]` gate would be wrong and where the choice belonged instead — "in store settings as an explicit seller choice". `store_settings.tracking_conversion_scope` (migration `086`, `TrackingConversionScope`) is that setting.

- **Default is `amazon_logistics_only`**, not `all`. Conversions are the metered, priced dimension now, a TB* number is the loudest supplier tell, and a native UPS/USPS scan is stronger evidence than a third-party carrier in an eBay Item-Not-Received case. A seller who wants maximum concealment opts up to `all` — and pays for it in quota. **Note this changed behaviour for existing rows**: an account already on the Aquiline provider converts only TB* numbers after `086`. That is intended (it lowers their cost and ours) and is reversible in one click.
- **`isAmazonLogisticsTracking(number, carrier)`** (`tracking-converter.ts`, pure + tested) is the single predicate. It was extracted from `LocalTrackingConverter.convertSync`, which already had to make exactly this call to pick the eBay carrier code — a second hand-written `TB` regex is how "convert only Amazon Logistics" quietly starts converting something the mapper labels UPS, or skipping something it labels Amazon. A test asserts the two always agree.
- **`store_settings.tracking_convert_manual_orders`** (default true) decides whether an order the seller linked BY HAND is also converted automatically. "Manual" is derived from `auto_fulfill_status !== PLACED` — the same rule `deriveFulfillmentState` uses — so no call site has to pass a flag and there is no second definition to drift. Default true because a seller who places every order by hand would otherwise have to remember a per-order button, and forgetting it exposes the supplier: the exact failure conversion exists to prevent.
- **`POST /v1/amazon/orders/:orderId/convert-tracking`** converts one order on demand — for a conversion the quota blocked at the time, a provider outage, an address that was incomplete when the shipment was first seen, or a seller who changed their mind. It goes THROUGH `resolveForOrder`, not around it: an already-converted order is returned as-is rather than paid for twice, a suspended account is refused, and the quota is spent normally. It bypasses only the two rules that exist to *infer* intent — the manual-orders switch and the carrier scope — because clicking the button on one order IS that intent. The UI offers it only when there is an Amazon tracking number and no conversion yet.
- **Bulk historical linking never triggers a conversion**, because `AmazonOrderSyncService` does not call the conversion service at all — conversion happens only at the shipped transition in `AmazonTrackingProcessorService`. A backlog of already-shipped orders that later converts is bounded by the same monthly quota, which degrades to pass-through rather than blocking.

- **eBay accepts `AQUILINE` — verified against a live eBay seller Add-Tracking dialog (2026-08-11), and it is what easync ships.** An earlier revision of this file and of `tracking-converter.ts` asserted the opposite ("eBay deprecated Bluecare/Aquiline validation"), sourced from secondary blog posts about a July-2024 change. That claim was **wrong** and it was load-bearing: it justified the pass-through as the only option and would have blocked this integration. Do not reintroduce it without a live eBay screenshot showing rejection.
- **A tracking number is still NEVER fabricated.** The no-fabrication rule stands and is unchanged: inventing a USPS/UPS number for a parcel that carrier never handled is fraud, and eBay scores the unscannable result against the seller. Aquiline is the opposite of fabrication — it issues a number it actually tracks and reports events for. `LocalTrackingConverter` remains the honest fallback for every failure.

**`TrackingConversionService` owns the decision, not the converter.** The `TrackingConverter` interface is now **async** (it was a sync pure function; an external provider is a paid network call). But the Aquiline path cannot live in a converter object at all — it needs the buyer address, the resolved seller profile, and it must persist its result. `AquilineTrackingConverter` is therefore a marker that **rejects** if called directly, so a caller bypassing the service fails loudly instead of quietly shipping the raw Amazon number.

- **Migration `074` is the correctness fix, not a convenience.** `orders.converted_tracking_number` / `converted_tracking_carrier` / `tracking_provider_shipment_id` / `tracking_provider` / `tracking_converted_at`. `handleShipped` deliberately rethrows when the eBay push fails so the next tick retries — with an unstored conversion that retry would **buy a second tracking number and hand eBay a different one**, leaving the buyer with a number that tracks nothing. A stored conversion short-circuits before any provider call, so a retry is free. The `Idempotency-Key` is derived from `(orderId, sourceTrackingNumber)` with no timestamp or random component, for the same reason.
- **`idx_orders_converted_tracking_number` is UNIQUE and partial.** The webhook arrives keyed only on the tracking number, so this is its lookup path; UNIQUE because one buyer's delivery event must never be able to complete another buyer's order.
- **Fail-soft everywhere.** Missing API key, exhausted plan quota, revoked key, incomplete buyer address, provider outage → local pass-through. A quota/auth failure logs at **error** (not warn) because it silently un-hides the supplier for every order — the exact thing the seller pays to avoid.

**Delivery detection is NEVER stopped by a converted tracking number (corrected 2026-08-23 — an earlier revision of this file said the opposite, and it was wrong).** The Aquiline **Integration API** (the provider surface this platform actually integrates against, superseding the v3 partner API these bullets originally described) has **no delivery webhook** — its events are `tracking.html.*` and `tracking.problem.*` only, never a `shipment.delivered`. `AmazonTrackingProcessorService` used to have a `hasWebhookDeliveryCoverage` gate that stopped the per-order Amazon scheduler once a conversion existed; that gate is **deleted** (locked by `tracking-webhook-coverage.guard.spec.ts`, a source-grep guard) because it would have stranded every converted order in SHIPPED forever. Polling now always continues at the shipped interval after a SHIPPED observation, via `scrapeOrderStatusWithTrackingHtml` (captures the order-status AND the ship-track page HTML in one rate-limiter slot — the provider wants that HTML roughly daily for an in-flight order).
  - **Every tick on an already-SHIPPED order re-uploads that HTML** (`TrackingConversionService.refreshTrackingHtml`), which is the recurring feed the provider derives carrier and delivery context from. It is gated on a STORED conversion (an unconverted order has nothing on the provider side to refresh), pushes nothing to eBay, re-runs no conversion, spends no quota, stamps `orders.tracking_html_uploaded_at`, and is fully best-effort. The condition is "the order was ALREADY SHIPPED coming into this tick", which covers the recurring feed AND the final upload on the delivered transition in one rule while never double-uploading — when the previous status was not SHIPPED, `handleShipped` did the upload itself as step ④ of the conversion.
  - **The scrape's ship-track URL is absolutized and persisted.** Amazon renders that anchor site-relative; `resolveTrustedAmazonTrackingUrl` resolves it against the marketplace origin and returns the trusted ABSOLUTE url (or nothing at all — never a relative one, which the provider rejects as `tracking_url_mismatch` after the conversion is already paid for). The processor then writes it to `orders.amazon_tracking_url`, the column `convertOnDemand` reads when it has no live page; before this only the manual-link path ever wrote it, so on-demand recovery was impossible for auto-fulfilled orders — exactly the ones that need it.
- **THE RAW AMAZON TRACKING NUMBER IS NEVER PUSHED TO eBAY WHEN A CONVERSION WAS EXPECTED (2026-09-01, operator decision — this reverses an earlier rule).** eBay's Fulfillment API has no update endpoint, so whatever the first `createShippingFulfillment` call sends is what the buyer sees forever. An earlier design pushed the raw number once a 12h window expired, arguing that visible-but-late tracking beat none — that traded the product's entire purpose (the seller pays to keep their supplier hidden) for a shipping-status timeliness nobody asked us to prioritise.

  `mayPushToEbay(outcome)` (`packages/shared`) is the single place the rule lives, and `ConversionOutcome` carries the distinction it needs:
  - `CONVERTED` → push. A real AQUA number.
  - `PASSTHROUGH_NOT_REQUIRED` → push. The seller's OWN choice: provider left on `local`, carrier outside their configured scope, or conversion switched off for hand-linked orders. The raw number is the intended result, and holding these would strand every order of every seller who never enabled conversion.
  - `PASSTHROUGH_FAILED` / `PASSTHROUGH_RETRYABLE` → **HOLD**. Conversion was expected and did not happen (suspended subscription, exhausted quota, missing API key, incomplete seller address, no usable profile, provider plan exhausted, or a transient failure). The order is not marked shipped on eBay at all.

  Splitting `PASSTHROUGH_TERMINAL` into `NOT_REQUIRED` vs `FAILED` is what makes this expressible — all eleven pass-through exits in `resolveForOrder` previously collapsed to one value, so "the seller chose not to convert" and "conversion broke" were indistinguishable.

  **The deferral window no longer decides whether to give up — nothing gives up.** `DEFERRAL_WINDOW_HOURS` (12h from `orders.shipped_detected_at`) now only decides how HARD to retry: hourly inside it, then back to the normal shipped cadence so a permanently stuck order is not scraped every hour forever. A genuine eBay API failure (401/5xx/network) still propagates exactly as before.

  **The cost is real and deliberate**: an order held past eBay's handling time earns a late-shipment defect and eventually an Item-Not-Received case. That is why `ActionCenterItemKey.ORDER_TRACKING_CONVERSION_HELD` is **CRITICAL** — detected structurally as `shipped_detected_at IS NOT NULL AND ebay_tracking_pushed_at IS NULL` past a 12h grace, so the count cannot drift from what the processor is actually doing. There is deliberately **no** "push the raw number anyway" override; the operator considered one and declined it.

  Recovery is the on-demand action: `convertOnDemand` now calls `AmazonTrackingQueueService.triggerImmediateTracking` after a successful conversion, so the held order pushes at once instead of waiting up to a day. It re-triggers the processor rather than pushing itself — the token refresh, line-item lookup and `ebay_tracking_pushed_number` write all live in `handleShipped`, and a second implementation is how the two drift.

  `raw-tracking-never-pushed.guard.spec.ts` locks all of it, including a source-grep for the removed argument, because the reversed rule is one line away and reads reasonable to anyone optimising for eBay defect metrics.

**NO CONVERSION HAS EVER RUN AGAINST THE REAL PROVIDER (status as of 2026-08-26).** An Aquiline **Starter** plan was purchased on 2026-08-23 (\$42/mo, 300 shipments, 10 seller profiles, \$0.14/shipment). What HAS been verified live: the base URL, the `Bearer {tokenId}.{tokenSecret}` auth scheme, the `GET /v1/me` response shape, the error body's machine-readable `code`, and that 15 rapid `GET`s draw no `429` and no rate-limit headers. What support then confirmed in writing (2026-08-26): the webhook envelope, the billing definitions, the absence of enforced rate limits, and that profiles are permanent.

What has still never happened is **a real conversion**. There is no sandbox, no live eBay order has been placed through the platform, and no `upsertOrders` / `uploadTrackingHtml` / `assign` sequence has run end to end. The four-call sequence, its response envelopes beyond `/v1/me` and `assign`, and every webhook code path are therefore written against a documented contract, not an observed one. Treat it the way the A2 auto-fulfill section treats its own "first real placement" gate: **do not describe any of it as working**, and confirm with the operator before claiming otherwise.

The provider's answers did remove the two largest unknowns: `reused: true` re-assigns are **not billed**, so the retry/deferral paths cannot double-charge; and the recurring HTML feed consumes **no** monthly usage, so §5.3 is free to run daily.

What that does and does not put at risk:
- **The decision layer IS exercised and testable without the provider** — scope (TB*-only vs all), the manual-order switch, entitlement, the monthly quota, the publishable-to-eBay guard, and the on-demand endpoint. Every one of those either returns the local pass-through or refuses, and none needs a network call to prove.
- **The unproven half is the four-call conversion sequence and its response shapes**: whether `upsertOrders` accepts our order body, whether Aquiline parses the ship-track HTML we upload, whether `assign` succeeds immediately after an `accepted` upload (the reason the bounded deferral exists), and the exact `AQUA…YQ` number it returns. All of it is isolated behind `AquilineClient`, and every failure degrades to the honest pass-through, so a wrong guess costs a conversion that does not happen — never a broken shipment or a wrong tracking number sent to a buyer.
- **The one thing to watch when it does go live** is whether the bounded deferral (see above) is long enough — `assign` failing to apply an `accepted` HTML upload for longer than `DEFERRAL_WINDOW_HOURS` would mean every deferred order falls through to the raw Amazon number anyway, silently reducing the conversion rate without erroring.

**Verification plan (first live order).** Connect a real eBay store in the test environment, place one low-value order end to end, and check in this order:
1. A profile appears in the Aquiline dashboard under `sh-{userId}-{marketplace}`, and `aquiline_profiles` holds the same id.
2. The order upserts and its ship-track HTML is `accepted` (`orders.aquiline_order_synced_at` / `tracking_html_uploaded_at` are stamped).
3. `assign` returns an `AQUA…YQ` number, `planUsed` increments by one, and the conversion is persisted to `orders.converted_tracking_number` BEFORE eBay is called.
4. eBay accepts the fulfillment under carrier `AQUILINE`, and `orders.ebay_tracking_pushed_number` records exactly what it received.
5. **Amazon polling CONTINUES** at `AMAZON_TRACKING_SHIPPED_INTERVAL_HOURS` — it is not supposed to stop, and an order that stops being polled after conversion is a defect, not the design. Each tick re-uploads fresh ship-track HTML, which is what keeps the provider's carrier and delivery context current.
6. **Delivery is detected by that continued polling**, never by a push. The Integration API has NO delivery webhook (its events are `tracking.html.*` and `tracking.problem.*` only), so the delivered transition comes from the same Amazon page visit that feeds the HTML, and only then is the per-order scheduler removed.
7. A `tracking.html.applied` webhook arrives and lands in `tracking_webhook_events` with `outcome = 'applied'`, and `tracking_webhook_raw_captures` shows `parsed_ok = true`. A `parsed_ok = false` row is the signal that the provider changed the envelope — read its `body` before touching the parser.

Only after all seven is the pass-through no longer the expected outcome.

### The webhook receiver (payload shape CONFIRMED 2026-08-26, migration `091`)

The provider supplied the envelope directly, because it **cannot** be in their OpenAPI document — that document is 3.0.3, which has no `webhooks:` section, so it can only publish event names and the signature header. Support's answer is therefore the sole authority for these fields; do not "correct" them against the spec, which is silent by construction.

```json
{ "event": "tracking.html.applied", "createdAt": "…", "data": { "profileId": "…", "orderId": "…", "outcome": "applied" } }
```

- `data` ALWAYS carries `profileId` + `orderId`, where **`orderId` is the MARKETPLACE order id** — the Amazon order id we sent to `upsertOrders`, matching `orders.amazon_order_id`. That is the join key.
- **The AQUA number is never in a webhook payload.** Anything needing it must read `GET /v1/profiles/{profileId}/orders/{orderId}` → `aquilineNumber`. This is why migration `091` drops `tracking_webhook_events.tracking_number`'s `NOT NULL`: the `075` schema made a real delivery structurally impossible to insert.
- Headers: `X-Webhook-Event` (the event name) and `X-Webhook-Signature: sha256=<hex>`, HMAC-SHA256 over the raw body. Signature verification was the one part of the original receiver the provider's answer confirmed rather than contradicted.
- Delivery: **4 attempts max** (1s / 5s / 20s, 8s timeout each), then dropped permanently. So the receiver must be idempotent AND fast — anything slower than 8s is retried, and after four tries the event is gone.
- `problemCode` appears on `tracking.problem.opened`, `tracking.html.rejected`, and OPTIONALLY on `tracking.html.accepted` when accepted in a degraded state. `tracking.problem.cleared` carries `previousProblemCode` instead.

**Only `tracking.html.applied` clears a problem, never a bare `accepted`.** The provider's own docs say never to treat success alone as applied; clearing on `accepted` would hide a fault that has not actually been fixed. `decideTrackingProblem` encodes this and is unit-tested against the provider's verbatim examples.

**An unknown problem code sets the flag but stores NULL.** The provider can add codes; a code we have no i18n key for must never reach a seller verbatim, and writing a raw string would do exactly that.

**Matching is scoped by profile, not by order id alone** — two sellers can legitimately hold the same Amazon order id, so `findOrder` joins `aquiline_profiles` to reach the right user. Matching on the order id alone would write one seller's problem onto another's order.

`orders.tracking_problem_code` feeds the Action Center's `ORDER_TRACKING_PROBLEM` item (WARNING, broken down by code). It matters more than its wording suggests: while a problem stands, the conversion falls back to the raw Amazon number, so the supplier the seller pays to hide is exposed and **nothing else in the flow reports it**.

Migration `090`'s `tracking_webhook_raw_captures` is retained: `TrackingWebhookController.captureRaw` still stores every signature-verified body verbatim before parsing, so a future shape change shows up as `parsed_ok = false` rather than as silence. Requires `AQUILINE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` and a publicly reachable URL (`pnpm --filter api aquiline:probe --register-webhook <url>`), neither of which exists from local dev.

**Historical note — `buildConversionBody` and `POST /v3/shipments` are GONE, and must not come back.** The original implementation targeted `developer.aquiline-tracking.com`'s **v3 partner API**, whose `POST /v3/shipments` is a real courier booking (`sender`, `recipient`, `parcels[{weightKg,lengthCm,…}]`, `serviceLevel`, `labelUrl`) with no property anywhere for an inbound tracking number — so it could never have converted a TBA number, and `buildConversionBody` guessed at several field names to try. Aquiline support confirmed on 2026-08-13 that this was the wrong surface entirely. The real one is the subscriber-only **Integration API** (`aquiline-tracking.com/app/api/integration`, `Authorization: Bearer {tokenId}.{tokenSecret}`), where a conversion is FOUR calls — profile → `orders/upsert` → `tracking-html` → `assign` — and the source signal is a ship-track PAGE URL plus its HTML, not a tracking-number field. `buildConversionBody`, the v3 base URL, the `X-API-Key` header and the delivery-webhook code built on the old belief are all deleted. Full contract: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-23-aquiline-integration-api-design.md`.

**Plan limits — CONFIRMED BY THE PROVIDER 2026-08-26. Only TWO of the three plan numbers constrain us, and the scary-looking one does not.**

- ***Aquiline shipments*** — the ONLY thing that consumes monthly plan usage. One unit = **one successful NEW `assign`** that issues an AQUA number. Starter's 300 is 300 new assigns per billing period. This is the meter our own `tracking_conversions_per_month` quota exists to stay under.
- ***Trackings*** — **not our meter at all.** The provider defines it as looking up tracking for shipments **created by other users**. Our own shipments, after assign, are not metered again: `upsertOrders`, `tracking-html` uploads, status updates, webhooks and `GET`s on our own orders all consume ZERO monthly usage. So the recurring HTML feed (§5.3) is free, and an earlier reading of this file — which treated "3,000 trackings" as a second ceiling to plan around and used it to argue continued polling was costly — was simply wrong.
- ***Seller profiles*** — 10/25/50/100/250 by tier, permanent, and **not exposed via the API**; only `GET /v1/profiles` (list) can be counted, which is what `AquilineProfileService` does. Note `X-Partner-Id` is v3 vocabulary and is NOT used by the Integration API.

**A re-assign that returns `reused: true` is FREE**, which is what makes the retry path safe: `withEbayRateLimitRetry`-style transport retries and the bounded deferral (§5.2) can both re-enter `assign` for an order that already has an AQUA number without being billed twice. This closes the double-billing question the design flagged as its main money exposure.

Capacity, corrected: the binding constraint is assigns, not trackings. At 500 users × ~50 orders/month that is ~25,000 assigns/month against 5,000 on the largest published tier, and ~500 profiles against a 250 ceiling — both still need a custom arrangement, but the "3,000 trackings" figure plays no part in that arithmetic.

**The usage window is the SUBSCRIPTION period, not the calendar month** (confirmed): `windowKey` is `currentPeriodStart`, and `used` resets on the anniversary (23 Aug → 23 Sep). Our own `QuotaEnforcementService` meters a UTC calendar month, so the two windows are offset by design — which is why `isPlanExhausted` reads the provider's own `planRemaining` from a TTL'd snapshot rather than inferring anything from our counters.

**Rate limits: none enforced today** (confirmed). No application-level 429 limiter, no separate limits for `assign` vs `tracking-html`, and the token is bound to one account so limits are de facto per account. The published "~100 req/min" was a pacing recommendation, not a hard limit; the provider's guidance for rollout is **1–2 req/sec sustained**. Infrastructure-level 429s remain possible on aggressive bursts, and `AquilineClient` already treats 429 as retryable TRANSPORT. No client-side limiter is built because the natural pacing is already far below that ceiling: HTML uploads ride the Amazon scrape, which `AMAZON_GLOBAL_CONCURRENCY` caps at 5 concurrent browser actions platform-wide. (The 250 req/min figure in their docs belongs to the separate v3 partner API, not this one.)

`store_settings.tracking_provider_profile_id` is vestigial — the profile now lives on `aquiline_profiles`, keyed by user × marketplace.

Key files: `tracking-converter.ts` (interface + local fallback), `tracking-conversion.service.ts` (orchestration, persistence, fail-soft), `aquiline.client.ts` (HTTP, typed `AquilineErrorKind`, bounded retry — 4xx never retried since a blind retry on the conversion path risks paying twice), `tracking-webhook.{controller,service,helpers}.ts` (+ `.helpers.spec.ts`).

### Amazon Scraping — Anti-Ban Strategy
- **Session isolation**: Each Amazon account gets its own browser state. **Legacy** (pre-A2): `.browser-state/{accountId}.json` storage-state files. **Current** (A2+): per-account persistent user-data-dir at `${BROWSER_STATE_DIR || $CWD/.browser-state}/profiles/{accountId}/` via `chromium.launchPersistentContext` — one full browser profile per account, cached in-process by `BrowserStateManager.getContext`. Legacy `.browser-state/{accountId}.json` sessions are migrated on first launch of the new profile via post-launch `context.addCookies(...)` (cookies only — localStorage intentionally NOT migrated; errors swallowed).
- **Idle eviction (I2 memory bound)**: each persistent context owns a Chromium process (~150–300 MB RSS). `BrowserStateManager` runs a periodic sweep (`BROWSER_CONTEXT_SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS`, default 2 min) and closes contexts that are **provably idle**: `context.pages().length === 0` **and** untouched longer than `BROWSER_CONTEXT_IDLE_TTL_MS` (default 10 min). The zero-page guard is non-negotiable — in-flight scrape/checkout always holds ≥1 page, so eviction cannot crash active work. Callers must still go through `AmazonRateLimiter.schedule(accountId, …)`.
- **Profile disk GC (2026-08-10) — the eviction above bounds MEMORY; this bounds DISK.** Nothing ever touched the `user_data_dir` itself, so a profile grew without limit (HTTP cache, code cache, service-worker CacheStorage, GPU/shader caches) and a deleted account leaked its profile forever: **50–250 GB at 500 accounts, with no cleanup at all**. `BrowserProfileGcService` (daily, first run 10 min after boot) closes that gap. Policy lives in the pure, Jest-covered `browser-profile-gc.ts`; the service is the fs shell.
  - **A cache prune never costs a login, and that distinction is the whole design.** Amazon's session is cookies (`at-main`/`sess-at-main`/`session-id`/`ubid-main`) in `Default/Network/Cookies`, decrypted with the key in the profile-root `Local State`. `CHROMIUM_DISPOSABLE_CACHE_PATHS` is an **allowlist** of browser-managed caches Chromium already evicts under disk pressure; `CHROMIUM_SESSION_CRITICAL_PATHS` names the session files, and the spec asserts no disposable entry **is or is an ancestor of** a critical one. That ancestor check is the real guard — listing `Default/Network` would take `Cookies` with it. Cost of a prune: one cache miss on the next page load. **Never add a path to the disposable list without re-reading that test.**
  - **Full removal is the rare branch, because a re-login can hit a captcha/OTP and take a buyer account out of service.** Only two cases reach it: an **orphan** (account gone from `amazon_accounts` — nothing to log back into, zero risk) and a profile **dormant** past `BROWSER_PROFILE_GC_DORMANT_DAYS` (default **90**; by then Amazon has usually expired the session server-side anyway, so the eviction is effectively free). `0` disables dormant eviction while keeping prune + orphan purge. `resolveBrowserProfileGcConfig` falls back to the **default** on an unparseable value and clamps to a 7-day floor — a typo must never become a mass re-login event.
  - **Three safety properties, each of which was a way to break an account**: (1) every mutation runs inside `AmazonRateLimiter.schedule(accountId, …)`, with a **liveness re-check inside the lock**, so a prune and a `launchPersistentContext` can never interleave on one `user_data_dir`; (2) a failed `amazon_accounts` read **aborts the sweep** — treating a DB outage as "no accounts exist" would purge all 500 profiles as orphans; (3) `hasLiveContext` outranks everything else. Reclaimed bytes are measured only for full removals: stat-ing every cache file across 500 profiles daily would cost minutes of IO for a log line.
  - Panel-tunable at `/admin` → Settings → **Data retention & disk** (`amazon.browserProfileGc.*`).
- **Fingerprint isolation**: Deterministic per-account fingerprint (user-agent, viewport, timezoneId from `hashCode(accountId)`) — same account always looks the same across restarts.
- **Proxy stack (self-service, per-account — replaced the platform-paid pool 2026-08-13).** The former model (a pool of fixed ISP proxies the platform bought and auto-assigned one-per-user, ~$3-4/proxy/30d — "the platform's largest recurring cost by a wide margin" per the earlier revision of this file) is retired. It does not scale: the cost is `active_users × $3-4/month` and the platform bore all of it, with no way to pass it to the seller who benefits. The replacement mirrors what competitors (easync) ship: **each `amazon_accounts` row carries its own OPTIONAL, user-supplied proxy** (migration `080` — `proxy_enabled`, `proxy_connection_type` (`http|https|socks5`, `ProxyConnectionType` enum in `packages/shared`), `proxy_host`, `proxy_port`, `proxy_username`, `proxy_password` encrypted AES-256-GCM `enc:`-prefixed same as the account's own Amazon password). Set from the **Amazon Account drawer** in the app (`AmazonAccountDrawer`, a "Proxy" section alongside the credential fields) — never from an admin panel or env var. If the user does not configure one, that account's browser automation runs **bare-IP**, and that is the expected default, not a degraded fallback: the platform bears zero proxy cost unless the user explicitly opts in and pays for their own.
  - **Granularity is per ACCOUNT now, not per user.** The old pool assigned one static IP per user (all of a user's Amazon buyer accounts shared it — the "one household, several accounts" pattern). A self-service proxy is a property of the account credentials themselves, so a user with two buyer accounts can give each its own proxy, or leave one bare and proxy only the other — their call, since they're paying for it.
  - **`BrowserStateManager.resolveProxy(accountId)`** reads the account's own row directly (no shared service, no cross-user claim/assignment logic to race) and returns `null` — bare-IP launch — whenever `proxy_enabled` is false, or host/port is missing, or the account doesn't exist, or the DB read fails. There is no platform fallback tier to fall through to.
  - **Auto-fulfill is NOT gated on a proxy.** `AmazonAccountsService.assertCanEnable` only requires a non-null spend cap now — `amazon.errors.autoFulfillCapRequired`. `AmazonCheckoutService.runForOrder`'s remaining proxy check (R2, `auto_fulfill_blocked_reason='proxy_required'`) fires ONLY when the account itself has `proxy_enabled=true` but the browser context failed to launch through it (`!browserState.isProxyActive(accountId)`) — i.e. it protects the user's own explicit choice from silently degrading to bare-IP, it does not enforce that a proxy must exist.
  - **The legacy `proxies` pool table and its env-template fallback (`PROXY_ENDPOINT`/`PROXY_USER`/`PROXY_PASS_TEMPLATE`/`PROXY_STRATEGY`) are gone from the code path entirely** — no service reads them. The DB table (migrations `057`/`058`) was **dropped by migration `094`** (2026-09-06).
- **Rate limiting** (Bottleneck via `AmazonRateLimiter.schedule(accountId, …)`):
  - Global: max `AMAZON_GLOBAL_CONCURRENCY` concurrent browser actions (default **5**, unchanged from when it was hardcoded). **This is a resource guard, not the anti-ban control** — bans are governed by the per-account limits below, which stay hardcoded; raising the global does not make any single account look busier. It is also the **platform throughput ceiling**: capacity is `maxConcurrent × 86,400` browser-seconds/day (5 ⇒ ~432k), and past ~400 buyer accounts it becomes the binding constraint. Lower `AMAZON_ORDER_SYNC_CRON` before raising it — that is far cheaper than the RAM (~150–300 MB RSS per unit).
  - Per-account: 1 concurrent, 3 seconds between requests, 20 requests/minute
  - Exponential backoff on 429/captcha/block responses
  - **Invariant**: callers MUST go through `AmazonRateLimiter.schedule(accountId, …)` so two `launchPersistentContext` calls never race the SingletonLock on the same user_data_dir.
- **Session reuse**: Existing cookies restored on repeat visits. Full login only when session expires.
- **Stealth**: `playwright-extra` + `puppeteer-extra-plugin-stealth` (patches navigator.webdriver, plugins, WebGL, etc.)
- **2FA support**: TOTP via `otplib` — user provides the secret key from Amazon's 2FA settings

### eBay Fulfillment API
- `createShippingFulfillment(accessToken, ebayOrderId, lineItemId, quantity, { trackingNumber, shippingCarrierCode })` — marks order as shipped on eBay
- Tracking number + carrier are mutually dependent — both must be provided together
- Currently forwards Amazon's real tracking number. Future: replace with generated fake tracking IDs for dropshipping.

### Buyer Auto-Messaging (settings-driven, optional)

Automated, template-driven buyer messages on four order lifecycle events. Configured per-store in Store Settings (global + per-eBay-store, same `store_settings` resolution as `amazonTaxRate`/`autoFulfillEnabled`). Per event: independent enable/disable + choose a predefined system template or a user-authored custom template. **No manual inbox/reply** — descoped; this is outbound-only automation.

- **Events**: `order_received` (thank-you on genuine order insert), `shipped` (on the tracking shipped transition — note eBay already auto-notifies on ship, so this is opt-in and the default template uses a distinct tone), `delivered` (on the delivered/completed transition), `feedback_request` (delayed BullMQ job, N days after delivered).
- **Config**: `store_settings.buyer_messaging` JSONB (`{ enabled, events: { [event]: { enabled, template: { kind: 'system'|'custom', id }, delayDays? } } }`). Row-level resolve (store row overrides global). Migration `054` adds the column + `buyer_message_templates` + `buyer_message_log`.
- **Templates are one unified, DB-backed, per-user space — no code-constant "system" templates (2026-08-08).** `buyer_message_templates` (migration `066` adds `is_default BOOLEAN`) holds every template a user can pick from, including their four starter defaults. `buyer_message_system_defaults` (migration `066`, PK `event_type`) is the DB-stored source copy for those defaults, replacing the old `SYSTEM_BUYER_MESSAGE_TEMPLATES` code constant — changing default wording is now a data change, not a deploy.
  - **Lazy per-user seeding, once.** `BuyerMessageTemplateRepository.ensureSeeded(userId)` (advisory-lock guarded, same "create on demand" idiom as `resolveProductData` in `listing-processor.service.ts`) inserts the four `is_default=true` rows from `buyer_message_system_defaults` the first time `GET /v1/buyer-messaging/templates` is called for a user with zero templates — never again after that, even if the user later deletes one, so a removed starter doesn't keep reappearing. Migration `066` also backfills existing users (skipping any event a user already has a row for) so it never ships with an empty list.
  - **A default is an ordinary template, not a protected one.** `isDefault: true` only drives a "Default" badge and the "Reset to default" action in the edit drawer — the row is fully editable/deletable/renamable like any other. `POST /v1/buyer-messaging/templates/:id/reset` restores just the `body` (not the name) from `buyer_message_system_defaults`, and only for a template where `is_default = true`.
  - **`BuyerMessageTemplateKind.SYSTEM` is legacy-only**, kept so a `store_settings.buyer_messaging` config saved before this change keeps resolving: `BuyerMessageService.resolveTemplate` reads its body from `buyer_message_system_defaults` by event type (DB), not the removed constant. New saves from the settings UI always write `CUSTOM` pointing at a real template row — the picker no longer distinguishes "system" vs "custom" sources.
- **Pipeline**: lifecycle seam → `buyer-message` BullMQ queue → `BuyerMessageProcessor` (idempotency guard via `buyer_message_log` partial-unique on `(ebay_order_id, event_type) WHERE status='sent'`; re-checks config at fire time → `skipped` if disabled; `redactForLog` strips tokens before any logged/thrown error; fail-soft `enqueue` never breaks order/tracking flows) → `BuyerMessagingProvider` port (`EbayMessageApiProvider`, eBay REST Message API `sendMessage`; token resolved per-account via `EbayService.getAccountAccessToken`).
- **Triggers**: `OrderSyncService` genuine-insert seam (`order_received`); `AmazonTrackingProcessorService` status transitions (`shipped`, `delivered` + delayed `feedback_request`). All env-gated (`BUYER_MESSAGING_ENABLED=true`, default off) + per-user config.
- **Env**: opt-in is per-user via Store Settings (master toggle + per-event) — there is NO env master switch. Only `BUYER_MESSAGING_FEEDBACK_DEFAULT_DELAY_DAYS=3` (default feedback-request delay).
- **FE — three surfaces, not one drawer.** `BuyerMessagingSection` (master toggle + 4 padded event cards, per-event template picker listing that user's own templates for the event, delayDays, shared hover/click/focus Tooltips explaining each event), mounted in `StoreSettingsDrawer` step 2. It is controlled by `StoreSettingsDrawer.container`; there is no nested Save — the drawer's final Save persists both ordinary store settings and the messaging config together through the existing two endpoints. Template management lives on the settings hub's "Store Configuration" card as two sibling drawers (mirrors the Listing Settings Groups Manage/Create split): `BuyerMessageTemplatesDrawer` (list — one merged grid of every template incl. defaults, event-type filter, full (untruncated) body preview per card, delete w/ `ConfirmModal`) and `BuyerMessageTemplateDrawer` (create/edit — name/event/body/placeholder-chips/preview, event type locked once created, "Reset to default" + confirm when `isDefault`). RTK Query: `storeSettingsApi` (config GET/PUT) + `buyerMessagingApi` (template CRUD + `resetBuyerMessageTemplate`). i18n under `storeSettings:storeSettings.messaging.*`.
- **Key files**: `apps/api/src/modules/buyer-messaging/` (`buyer-messaging.module.ts`, `buyer-message.service.ts`, `buyer-message.processor.ts`, `buyer-message-queue.service.ts`, `buyer-message.provider.ts`, `buyer-message-template.repository.ts` (+ `ensureSeeded`/`resetToDefault`/`getSystemDefaultBody`), `buyer-message.controller.ts`, `buyer-message-helpers.ts` + `.spec.ts`, `buyer-messaging.constants.ts`). Shared: `packages/shared/src/domain/buyer-messaging/`, `packages/shared/src/schemas/buyer-messaging/`. The `buyer-message` queue is registered in the admin queue registry (`ADMIN_QUEUE_NAMES`, `OBSERVED_QUEUE_NAMES`).
- **Known follow-ups (deferred minors from implementation)**: the eBay Message API `sendMessage` exact field shape is `// TODO(confirm)` in the provider (port-isolated, verified against live docs at runtime).

### Key Files
- **Backend orders**: `src/modules/orders/` — OrdersService, OrderSyncService (owns `recomputeProfit` + `maybeEnqueueAutoFulfill`), EbayFulfillmentService, OrderSyncQueueService, OrderSyncProcessorService, **AutoFulfillQueueService** (A2 producer). Pure helpers: `profit-calculation.ts` (`computeNetProfit`, `deriveCostCaptureStatus`, `estimateProvisionalNetProfit`, `deriveProfitBasis`), `*.spec.ts` unit tests.
- **Backend Amazon**: `src/modules/amazon/` — AmazonAccountsService (owns `assertCanEnable` A2 guard + self-service proxy field read/write/encryption), AmazonScrapingService, AmazonOrderParserService, AmazonTrackingQueueService, AmazonTrackingProcessorService (wires the tracking converter), AmazonRateLimiter, BrowserStateManager (persistent context + per-account self-service proxy resolution), **AmazonOrderSyncService + AmazonOrderSyncQueueService + AmazonOrderSyncSchedulerService + AmazonOrderSyncProcessor** (auto cost-capture), **order-matcher.ts / pick-best-match.ts** (pure match heuristic + tests), **AmazonCheckoutService + AutoFulfillProcessor + auto-fulfill-helpers.ts (.spec.ts)** (A2 checkout/processor/pure helpers), **tracking-converter.ts (.spec.ts)** (`LocalTrackingConverter` + `ApiTrackingConverter` stub + `resolveConverter`). (`proxy.service.ts`, the platform-pool manager, was deleted 2026-08-13.)
- **Backend products**: `src/modules/products/` — ProductsService (price/image lookup, `decrementStock` for sale-driven stock sync)
- **Product refresh pipeline** (Keepa sole provider): `keepa.service.ts`, `refresh-scheduler.service.ts`, `refresh-processor.service.ts`, `keepa-usage.service.ts`, `product-sync.service.ts` — see "Product Refresh Pipeline" section above.
- **Sale-driven stock sync**: `src/modules/orders/stock-sync-queue.service.ts` (producer) + `src/modules/listings/stock-sync-processor.service.ts` (consumer) + `ProductSyncService.syncListingsForProduct()` + `ListingStrategyService.calculateQuantity()` (canonical quantity formula)
- **Frontend orders**: `apps/web/src/features/orders/` — list page, detail page, Amazon linking
- **Frontend Amazon**: `apps/web/src/features/amazon/` — accounts page, linking modal, RTK Query API
- **Shared**: `packages/shared/src/domain/orders/`, `packages/shared/src/domain/amazon/`, `packages/shared/src/schemas/orders/`, `packages/shared/src/schemas/amazon/`

## Architectural Rules

1. **Type Safety** — Types in `packages/shared/src/domain/`. No `any`. No duplicates.
2. **Validation** — Zod schemas in `packages/shared/src/schemas/`. Backend DTOs use `class-validator` but implement shared interfaces.
3. **UI & Styling** — Emotion with semantic tokens. No inline styles. No hardcoded colors (`#fff`, `rgba(...)`, etc.), spacing (`16px`, etc.), or magic numbers. Everything must be a theme token via `tkn()`. If a new color/spacing/value is needed, add it to `packages/ui/src/theme/` (designTokens, themes, tkn paths) first. Use `Icon` component, no inline SVGs.
4. **Forms** — Use `ModernTextInput` molecule. Inputs must use `React.forwardRef` and `value={value ?? ''}`.
5. **Loading & Errors** — `useLoading(isLoading)` for global UI overlay. `showMessage` from `UIContext` for errors. Loading state is business logic — it belongs in container files (`.container.tsx`), never in component files (`.component.tsx`). Component files must NOT render loading spinners or conditional loading UI; the container handles loading via the `useLoading` hook which shows a global overlay. The only exception is passing an `isLoading` prop to a Button atom for inline button spinner.
6. **Container/Component Split (strict, enforced by ESLint + PreToolUse hook)** — Every feature component is split into 4 files:
   - `[Name].component.tsx` — JSX markup only. Allowed hooks: `useTranslation`, `useTheme`. Forbidden: `useState`, `useEffect`, `useMemo`, `useCallback`, `useReducer`, `useRef`, RTK Query hooks (`useQuery`/`useMutation`/`useLazyQuery`), `useSelector`, `useDispatch`, `useNavigate`, formatters (`formatCurrency`, etc.), event handlers. Forbidden: `styled(...)`.
   - `[Name].container.tsx` — All logic. Forbidden: `styled(...)`. JSX is allowed only as `<Component .../>` return.
   - `[Name].style.ts` — All `styled(...)` calls.
   - `[Name].types.ts` — `interface`/`type`/`enum` declarations only.

   **Atoms/Molecules** (`packages/ui/src/{atoms,molecules}/`) follow the same rules. **Stateful** atoms/molecules (Select, Dropdown, Tooltip, etc.) MUST split into `.container.tsx` + `.component.tsx` like features. **Stateless** atoms/molecules (Button, Badge) stay as `.component.tsx` + `.style.ts` + `.types.ts`.

   **Exempt:** `apps/web/src/features/landing/**`, `apps/web/src/**/api/*.ts(x)`, `apps/web/src/app/store.ts`, `apps/api/**`, `*.config.{ts,js,mjs,cjs}`.

   Enforced by `design-system/styled-only-in-style-files`, `design-system/types-only-in-types-files`, `design-system/logic-only-in-container`, `design-system/no-styled-in-container` ESLint rules, and by `.claude/hooks/pretooluse-frontend-rules.js` (Write/Edit/MultiEdit).
7. **Shared Utilities** — Reusable formatting and locale utilities (e.g., `formatCurrency`, `formatCompactNumber`, `formatDate`, `getLocaleConfig`) live in `packages/ui/src/utils/`. Import from `@repo/ui`. Never define them locally in component or container files.
8. **Design System Only (no custom UI primitives)** — All UI primitives (inputs, selects, checkboxes, buttons, modals, dropdowns, toggles, date pickers, etc.) MUST come from `packages/ui` (atoms or molecules). Never use native HTML elements (`<select>`, `<input>`, `<button>`, etc.) or build custom form controls directly in feature code. If a needed component doesn't exist in the design system, create it there first as an atom/molecule, then use it. This ensures consistency and reusability across all screens.
9. **MessageModal for success/error/info/warning messages** — All informational and error messages MUST use the `MessageModal` molecule via `showMessage` from `UIContext`. Never use native `alert()`/`confirm()`, create custom modal implementations, or bypass this pattern. `MessageModal` provides consistent styling with type-appropriate icons (success=check-circle, error=x-circle, warning=alert-triangle, info=info) and proper button handling.
10. **No hardcoded status/constant strings** — All status values, type discriminators, and constant strings MUST be defined as enums in `packages/shared/src/domain/`. Never use string literals like `'active'`, `'draft'`, `'custom'`, `'predefined'`, etc. directly in code. Use the corresponding enum (e.g., `ListingStatus.ACTIVE`, `TemplateType.CUSTOM`, `PolicyType.PAYMENT`, `OrderStatus.SHIPPED`, `EbayAccountStatus.ACTIVE`). If a new constant is needed, create or extend an enum in the shared package first.
12. **No hardcoded UI strings — all text via i18n** — Every user-visible string (labels, buttons, messages, placeholders, tooltips, table headers, empty states, counts, etc.) MUST use i18n `t()` with keys defined in `packages/shared/src/i18n/resources/{en,tr}/`. Never write raw text like `<Text>Clear</Text>` or `{listings.length} results` directly in JSX. The only exceptions are: (a) universal symbols like `—` (em dash) used as empty-value placeholders, (b) numeric/format values produced by formatters (`formatCurrency`, `formatDate`, etc.), and (c) string literals passed to `t()` as keys. If a new string is needed, add the key to **both** `en/` and `tr/` JSON files first, then use `t('namespace.key')` in the component.
11. **Keep CLAUDE.md up to date** — When changes are made that affect project conventions, architecture, commands, patterns, or rules, CLAUDE.md MUST be updated to reflect the current state. This includes new packages, changed file paths, updated tooling, new architectural patterns, or modified workflows. Do not let CLAUDE.md become stale.
13. **Mobile-Web Responsiveness** — Every new page, feature, or component must render correctly down to a 360px-wide viewport. Use `theme.breakpoints.*` (`sm`/`md`/`lg`/`xl` and their `*Below` complements) via `tkn('breakpoints.*')` — never a hardcoded `@media` literal or a raw `window.innerWidth` comparison (for the rare JS-side, non-CSS threshold, use an exported constant like `SIDEBAR_MOBILE_BREAKPOINT_PX` — never a second inline magic number). Follow the codebase's existing patterns:
    - Multi-column layouts collapse to a single column at `breakpoints.md`/`mdBelow` (see `SettingsHubPage.style.ts` `TwoColGrid`, `ListingDetailPage.style.ts` `SectionGrid`).
    - Card grids use `DataTable.style.ts`'s `GridContainer` `minmax(min(100%, ...), 1fr)` pattern, never a fixed `repeat(N, 1fr)`.
    - Wide/tabular content that can't reflow gets its own local `overflow-x: auto` container (see `Table.style.ts` `OverflowWrapper`, `ActionCenterPage.style.ts` `Toolbar`) — never left to overflow the page.
    - A horizontal image+content card must stack to `flex-direction: column` below `breakpoints.sm`/`smBelow` once fixed-width children can't coexist with reflowing content at that width (see `OrderCard.style.ts`).
    - Overlays follow `Drawer`/`Modal`'s existing mobile behavior: `Drawer` goes full-width below `breakpoints.md`; a multi-button `Modal` footer stacks to full-width buttons below `breakpoints.md`, keeping the same visual order as desktop.
    - Popover-style menus (`Dropdown`, `Select`) render as a bottom sheet below 640px width, not a shrunk floating popover — see `Select`'s `isMobile`/`createPortal`/`Overlay`+`BottomSheet` pattern, mirrored by `Dropdown`. Reuse that pattern for any new popover/menu atom rather than a third implementation.
    - Fixed bottom action bars reserve `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` in their bottom padding (see `MobileActionBar` in `ListingDetailPage.style.ts`/`OrderDetailsPage.style.ts`).
    - `apps/web/src/index.css` carries a global `overflow-x: hidden` safety net on `html`/`body` — do not rely on it instead of giving wide content its own scroll container; it exists to catch accidents, not as a first line of defense.
    - Verify at a 375px-wide viewport (browser devtools device emulation) before calling a UI change done, per the existing "test in a real browser" guidance above.

## Environment Setup

1. `pnpm docker:up` (PostgreSQL on :5432, Redis on :6379)
2. Copy `apps/api/.env.example` to `apps/api/.env` and fill in values
3. Copy `apps/web/.env.example` to `apps/web/.env` (defaults work for local dev)
4. `pnpm dev`

API: `http://localhost:3000` (Swagger at `/api/docs`). Web: Vite dev server (default :5173).

## System Requirements & Deployment (local / test / prod)

SellerHill runs the same code in every environment — only config (env vars) and capacity differ. Three runtimes: **local** (developer machine), **test** (Coolify on a VPS), **prod**. There is no environment-specific code path.

### Shared stack (all environments)

| Component | Purpose | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **PostgreSQL 16** | primary DB (`pg` driver, no ORM) | schema in `docker/postgres/init.sql` + `apps/api/migrations/`; auto-run on API boot |
| **Redis 7** | BullMQ job queues | `order-sync`, `stock-sync`, `amazon-*`, `auto-fulfill`, `keepa-refresh`, etc. |
| **Node 20+ / pnpm 9+** | api + web + packages | monorepo workspace |
| **Playwright Chromium** | Amazon scraping + A2 checkout | headless; needs OS libs (see below) |
| **(A2) Self-service proxy** | Amazon anti-ban | Optional, per Amazon buyer account, user-supplied from the Amazon Account drawer (migration `080`). Not platform-provided — no admin provisioning, no env fallback. |

**Playwright/Chromium OS deps** (Linux servers, not needed on local Docker which bundles them): `libnss3 libatk1.0-0 libatk-bridge2.0-0 libcups2 libxkbcommon0 libxcomposite1 libxdamage1 libxfixes3 libxrandr2 libgbm1 libpango-1.0-0 libcairo2 libasound2`. Install once: `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium`.

### Local dev

- **Machine:** your PC. **8 GB RAM minimum** (Chromium for scraping is the heaviest single process).
- **Services:** `pnpm docker:up` runs PostgreSQL + Redis + pgAdmin in Docker — nothing else to install.
- **A2 here:** runs bare-IP by default (no proxy required to enable auto-fulfill). To exercise scraping/checkout through a proxy, enter one in the Amazon Account drawer's Proxy section for a real Amazon buyer account, then use **dry-run mode** on the account.
- No GPU needed (A2 uses Playwright CPU; local LLM is a separate B-spec concern).

### Test (Coolify / VPS)

- **Current Hostinger VPS:** 4 vCPU, 16 GB RAM, 200 GB disk, **no GPU** — runs api + web + postgres + redis + Playwright. This is adequate for test.
- Run Postgres + Redis as containers (Coolify stack or `docker-compose`). api + web as Coolify services behind the Coolify reverse proxy.
- If you want scraping ban-resistance or want to test A2 dry-run/live checkout through a proxy, configure one per Amazon buyer account from its Account drawer — there is nothing to provision at the platform/admin level anymore.
- Add real Amazon buyer accounts (encrypted at rest via `AMAZON_ENCRYPTION_KEY`).
- 16 GB is comfortable for a handful of accounts; the I2 idle-eviction (`BROWSER_CONTEXT_IDLE_TTL_MS`) bounds resident Chromium so even ~10–20 accounts won't pile up.

### Prod

- **PostgreSQL 16** (managed recommended) + **Redis 7**, both with persistence + backups.
- **API + Web** behind a reverse proxy (Coolify / Nginx / Caddy) with TLS.
- **CPU:** 4+ vCPU (Playwright is CPU-bound; the per-account rate limiter caps concurrency, but headroom matters).
- **RAM — the key dimension, driven by concurrent Amazon accounts:** ~150–300 MB per resident Chromium context + ~1–1.5 GB base (api + web + Postgres + Redis). I2 idle-eviction means resident contexts are bounded by **active** concurrency, not total account count. **Minimum 16 GB; 32 GB for multi-tenant scale.** Set OOM/alerting on memory.
- **Disk:** every large consumer is now TTL-bounded, but size for the steady state, not the average. At ~500 accounts: Postgres 60–100 GB in year 1 (mostly `products.raw_keepa_data`, with the append-only logs held by the `data-retention` job), `.browser-state/profiles/` bounded by `BrowserProfileGcService` (unbounded before 2026-08-10 — it was the single largest consumer at 50–250 GB), `fulfillment-evidence/` ~15 GB at the 7-day TTL, Loki ~10–20 GB at 7 days, `apps/api/logs/` a few GB gzipped. **Budget 1 TB NVMe at 500 accounts**; 50 GB only covers a handful.
- **Proxying is optional and self-service** — a seller who wants it enters their own proxy on the Amazon Account drawer per buyer account. The platform provisions and pays for nothing; there is no pool to size to the active-user count.
- **GPU:** not required and not planned — Playwright is CPU-bound and the LLM is a hosted API call.

### Secrets / env checklist (per environment)

- `AMAZON_ENCRYPTION_KEY` — 32-byte hex (AES-256-GCM for buyer-account credentials). **Required wherever amazon_accounts are used.**
- JWT/auth secrets (access + refresh-cookie signing).
- `KEEPA_API_KEY` (product refresh pipeline).
- eBay app credentials (per connected store).
- `CORS_ORIGINS` / `COOKIE_DOMAIN` / `COOKIE_SAMESITE` (prod web origin). **`main.ts` falls back through `CORS_ORIGINS` → legacy `CORS_ORIGIN` → `FRONTEND_URL` → `http://localhost:5173` (2026-08-15)** — an empty `CORS_ORIGINS` used to resolve straight to the localhost default, rejecting every real browser origin with "Origin '...' is not allowed by CORS" (hit live in test registration, twice: once from the compose file defaulting `CORS_ORIGINS` to an unset value, again after that fix because Coolify's env panel had a `CORS_ORIGINS` row present but left **blank** — an explicitly-empty env var still reaches `process.env` as `''`, and Coolify can inject its panel variables straight into the container, which can bypass a compose-file-level `${CORS_ORIGINS:-${FRONTEND_URL}}` default entirely). The fallback therefore lives in the app itself, not only in the compose files' matching `${CORS_ORIGINS:-${FRONTEND_URL}}` default (`docker-compose.test.yml`/`docker-compose.production.yml`) — the two are redundant on purpose, since the compose-level default is cheap insurance but the code-level one is what actually cannot be bypassed by however a given PaaS assembles the container's environment.
- A2 tunables (all optional, defaults safe): `AUTO_FULFILL_*`, `BROWSER_CONTEXT_*`, `FULFILLMENT_EVIDENCE_*`, Keepa refresh tunables.

**Env vs admin panel.** The list above is the complete set of things that MUST be env: connection bootstrap, crypto keys, provider credentials, process identity. Operational tuning (Keepa refresh cadence + kill switch, AI content toggle, quota enforcement, SMTP host/user/password, order-matching tolerances, evidence retention, admin warning thresholds) is edited at `/admin` → Settings and stored in `platform_settings`; env values there are only the fallback when no override row exists. Prefer the panel — it needs no redeploy and is audit-logged.

**Compose files.** `docker-compose.yml` = local dev services (Postgres/Redis/pgAdmin/Loki/Promtail/Grafana — no LLM service; see "Provider/config"). `docker-compose.test.yml` = Coolify test/sandbox (eBay sandbox endpoints, debug logging, concurrency 1). `docker-compose.production.yml` = Coolify production (eBay production endpoints). The two Coolify files build the same images from the same code and differ only in configuration; both mount `browser_state` + `fulfillment_evidence` + `api_logs` volumes, define a `/api/health/live` healthcheck, and carry a memory limit sized for resident Chromium contexts.

### A2 operational checklist (before enabling real-money auto-fulfill in any environment)

1. (Optional) If the account should route through a proxy, configure host/port/credentials in its Amazon Account drawer and confirm it's reachable — auto-fulfill no longer requires this to enable, but a misconfigured *enabled* proxy still blocks with `proxy_required` rather than silently running bare-IP.
2. ≥1 Amazon buyer account with `auto_fulfill_enabled=true` + `auto_fulfill_cap_total` set.
3. **Run `auto_fulfill_dry_run=true` first** — confirms the full checkout flow reaches the Amazon review step and the cap behaves, **without charging**. Inspect `fulfillment-evidence/{ebayOrderId}/dry_run_review-*.png`.
4. **Tune the Playwright selectors** in `AmazonCheckoutService` against live Amazon DOM during dry-run (selectors are best-effort and DOM-drift is the main fragility). Keep selectors in the `CHECKOUT_SELECTORS` constant.
5. Only then flip `auto_fulfill_dry_run=false` on a low-value test order and watch the placed/blocked status + evidence.
6. Monitor: BullMQ queue depth (`auto-fulfill`, `amazon-tracking`), `orders.auto_fulfill_status` (watch `blocked`/`failed`), memory (Chromium).

### A2 enablement status & gating (read this before touching auto-fulfill)

> **State as of 2026-07-30 (commit `40906aa`): dry-run selector tuning is DONE.** Dry-run walks the **full** Amazon checkout to the Place Order button and stops there, against a real logged-in buyer session through a proxy. **Do not describe the dry-run gate as open** — an earlier revision of this section (2026-07-20) said "no live checkout has ever been run", and that sentence outlived the work by ten days and misled a later reader. The one thing still unrecorded is the **first real placement** (see "Remaining gate" below).

**Getting there is what produced most of the hardening documented above, and each defect was hiding the next one** — which is why it took many runs, and why the diagnostic split (`review_unreadable` ≠ `cap`, `no_asin` ≠ `out_of_stock`) exists at all:
- **Authentication was never proven, only assumed.** `performLogin` marked an account ACTIVE whenever a submit produced no error, and `isSessionValid` accepted any URL without `/signin` — but Unified Auth serves credential pages under `/ax/claim/*`, so signed-out sessions passed both. Checkout then ran on a signed-out page, silently no-opping every step and failing only at the review-total read, **reported as `cap`** — pointing the operator at the spend guard when the real problem was the login. This is the origin of `probeAmazonAuth` / `decideAmazonAuth` and of `review_unreadable` being a distinct reason. See "Amazon authentication must be positively PROVEN".
- **A destructive-action guard was missing**: Amazon's create-account form was being filled whenever it did not recognise an email — one submit from creating an unintended Amazon account under the customer's own address. It is now never filled.
- **Wrong-recipient guards** (ship-to must be the eBay BUYER, not the buyer account holder) and the street+zip address matcher come from live observations: a household with two addresses under one zip, and Amazon prefilling a ZIP from the session's delivery location (observed `10020` where `20500` was intended). See "Automated Amazon Fulfillment (A2)" step 5.

Selectors remain the standing fragility: `CHECKOUT_SELECTORS` is written against Amazon's DOM, which Amazon rotates and which differs by account/region. **Re-run dry-run after a suspected DOM drift, or before onboarding a buyer account in a new region** — it is the cheap way to prove the selectors still resolve, and `dry_run` also simulates the post-purchase bookkeeping so you can see what a placement does to an order without money leaving.

**Remaining gate: the first REAL placement.** Nothing in git or in this file records an order actually placed with `auto_fulfill_dry_run=false`, so treat it as not yet done and **confirm with the operator before claiming otherwise**. The step: flip `auto_fulfill_dry_run=false` on ONE account, set a tight `auto_fulfill_cap_total` (e.g. `$10`), let one low-value matched order through, and watch `auto_fulfill_status` reach `placed` with a real `amazon_order_id` and a trusted (`linked`) `net_profit`. Only then widen caps and enable further accounts.

**Standing prerequisite (enforced in code, not convention):**
1. **A real Amazon buyer account** (email + password + 2FA secret) with `auto_fulfill_enabled=true` and `auto_fulfill_cap_total` set. TOTP is answered from the stored secret — fulfillment must never require a human to type a code.

**Historical note — proxying used to be a hard prerequisite here and no longer is (2026-08-13).** The 2026-07-30 dry-run ran through a platform-provisioned fixed ISP proxy under the pool model that existed at the time (`assertCanEnable` refused to enable auto-fulfill without one). That pool is retired — see "Amazon Scraping — Anti-Ban Strategy". Proxying is now optional and self-service per Amazon account; nothing blocks enabling auto-fulfill on an account with no proxy configured, and it will run bare-IP. If you want the historical dry-run's ban-resistance, configure a proxy on the account before onboarding it, but it is no longer required to reach the review step or place an order.

**How to guide the operator:**
- **Wants to test the flow** → dry-run is the tool, and it is known-working. Set `auto_fulfill_dry_run=true`, let one matched order through, read `orders.auto_fulfill_status` (expect `dry_run`) plus `fulfillment-evidence/{ebayOrderId}/`. A blocked reason other than `cap` means that step's selectors need a live-DOM patch.
- **Wants to go live** → the remaining gate above, one account and a tight cap first.
- **Reports auto-fulfill "not working"** → check the blocked reason before anything else, and trust the taxonomy: `review_unreadable` is a selector break, `cap` is the spend guard actually firing, `no_asin` is a delisted product (fix or end the listing — it is not stock that returns), `proxy_required` is that account's OWN configured proxy failing to apply (not a platform pool issue — there is no pool anymore; check the account's proxy fields). That separation exists precisely because these were once all reported as `cap`.
- **Never remove the dry-run step from onboarding docs.** The review-step hard cap is only as good as `readReviewGrandTotal`; if that selector misses, the check fails closed (`cap`) — safe, but it means auto-fulfill is silently not running.

### Observability — log tracing (Loki/Promtail/Grafana)

A free, self-hosted log-tracing stack, present in **every** environment (local `docker-compose.yml` and both Coolify compose files) so a request/job can be traced end-to-end by `correlationId` — the id already stamped onto every `apps/api` log line by `RequestIdMiddleware` → AsyncLocalStorage → Winston's `correlationFormat` (see `apps/api/src/common/observability/`).

- **Why file-tailing, not container-log scraping**: locally `apps/api` runs on the host via `pnpm dev:api`, not in a container, so there is no API container stdout for Promtail to scrape. Instead Winston writes JSON logs to `apps/api/logs/*.log` (`logger.config.ts` — the two `winston-daily-rotate-file` transports now run in **every** environment, not just `NODE_ENV=production` as before; `LOG_LEVEL` is also now actually honored, previously a silent no-op) and Promtail tails that directory. Local dev bind-mounts the host folder (`./apps/api/logs`); the Coolify files share a named `api_logs` volume between the `api` and `promtail` services (`api`'s WORKDIR is `/app/apps/api`, so the same relative `logs/` path lands in the shared volume). One `docker/promtail/promtail-config.yml` is reused everywhere via `-config.expand-env=true` + an `ENVIRONMENT` env var set per compose file (`development`/`test`/`production`).
- **Label design**: only `level` (bounded: debug/info/warn/error) plus the static `job`/`environment` labels are indexed in Loki. `correlationId`/`queueName`/`jobId`/`context` are parsed by Promtail's `json` pipeline stage into queryable-but-unindexed fields — putting a high-cardinality id straight into a Loki label is a known anti-pattern that wrecks the index.
- **Local dev**: `pnpm docker:up` starts `sellerhill_loki` (`:3100`), `sellerhill_promtail`, `sellerhill_grafana` (`http://localhost:3001`, `admin`/`admin123` — hardcoded like pgAdmin's local-dev credentials) alongside the existing services; no `pnpm docker:*` script changes were needed since they run plain `docker-compose` with no `-f` filter. Grafana auto-provisions the Loki datasource and a starter "SellerHill API Logs" dashboard (`docker/grafana/provisioning/`) — nothing to click through.
- **Trace a request**: grab the `X-Request-ID` response header (or read it straight from a log line), then in Grafana → Explore → Loki: `{job="sellerhill-api"} | json | correlationId="<id>"`.
- **Coolify (test/production)**: same three services added, on the `coolify` network. **Grafana publishes no host port** (neither does `api`/`web` in these files — Coolify's own domain/proxy config is how a service becomes reachable), and `GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` is a **required** Coolify env var (no hardcoded prod password). Reach it via an SSH tunnel to the VPS; adding a public Coolify domain + auth for it is a deliberate follow-up, not wired up here.
- **Config is baked into the image at build time in the two Coolify compose files, NOT bind-mounted (2026-08-14).** `loki`/`promtail`/`grafana` used to be `image: grafana/...` + a bind mount of `./docker/{loki,promtail,grafana}/...` from the Coolify host's git checkout — the same pattern `nginx.conf`/`api-entrypoint.sh` already avoid via `COPY` in `Dockerfile.web`/`Dockerfile.api`. That bind-mount source repeatedly broke redeploys with `runc create failed: ... not a directory`: Docker auto-creates a directory when a bind-mount source doesn't exist yet (e.g. mid a partial/interrupted deploy), and Coolify's checkout never runs `git clean -fd`, so the stale directory survives every subsequent `git reset --hard` and permanently shadows the real file. **Fixed by giving each of the three its own tiny `Dockerfile`** (`docker/loki/Dockerfile`, `docker/promtail/Dockerfile`, `docker/grafana/Dockerfile` — each just `FROM <upstream image>` + `COPY <config> <path>`) and switching the compose service from `image:` to `build: { context: ./docker/<service> }`. The config files themselves did not move; only how the container gets them changed. Data volumes (`loki_data`, `grafana_data`, `promtail_positions`, `api_logs`) are untouched — this only affects the read-only config mounts. **`docker-compose.yml` (local dev) is deliberately NOT changed** — the local bind mount is the same repo working directory Docker Compose already reads from, so the desync this fixes cannot happen there, and bind-mounting keeps local config edits live without a rebuild.
- **Retention**: Loki filesystem storage with compactor-based retention, 7 days by default (`docker/loki/loki-config.yml`, `limits_config.retention_period`) — raise it there if you need longer history.
- Key files: `docker/loki/{Dockerfile,loki-config.yml}`, `docker/promtail/{Dockerfile,promtail-config.yml}`, `docker/grafana/{Dockerfile,provisioning/{datasources,dashboards}/}`, `apps/api/src/common/config/logger.config.ts`.

### Backups & monitoring (prod)

- **Postgres:** daily logical backup (pg_dump) + point-in-time if managed.
- **`fulfillment-evidence/`:** ephemeral, TTL-cleaned — not backed up.
- **`.browser-state/profiles/`:** ephemeral session profiles — not backed up (re-login on loss). Disk-bounded by `BrowserProfileGcService`; watch its nightly summary line for orphan/dormant removals, since each one means an account will re-authenticate.
- **Alert on:** API OOM / restart, BullMQ dead-letter growth, spike in `auto_fulfill_status='blocked'`.

## Figma Redesign — Per-Theme Tokens

**Dark mode is disabled app-wide for phase 1 (2026-08-14).** `packages/ui/src/context/ThemeContext.tsx`'s `ThemeProvider` now hardcodes `themeMode` to `'light'` — no `localStorage` read, no `prefers-color-scheme` detection, `toggleTheme`/`setThemeMode` are no-ops. The header theme-toggle button (`AppLayout`, `OperatorLayout`) and the unused `ThemeToggle` molecule were removed outright. Reason: dark mode had never been tested end-to-end, and the system-preference detection was silently dropping visitors with an OS dark preference into an unverified theme on their very first screen (most visibly on the landing→app transition, since the landing page forces its own light theme via a nested provider — see "Landing page" above — while the app's own provider used to pick up the OS preference independently). `darkTheme` and every per-theme token below are untouched and still correct; re-enable by restoring `getInitialTheme()`'s localStorage/system-preference logic and re-adding a toggle control once dark mode is actually verified — do not re-derive this from scratch.

The figma Make redesign (https://sweet-yang-69529706.figma.site/) introduced tonal shifts:
- **Primary is per-theme**: light uses `#2563eb` (blue-600), dark uses `#6366f1` (indigo-500). Do NOT expect them to match.
- **Sidebar background is per-theme**: light deep blue `#0c1f52`, dark near-black `#0d0f18`.
- **Borders are alpha-based**: `#00000014` (light) / `#ffffff12` (dark) — not solid hex.
- **`accent` token category** (emerald `#10b981`) is for "Active" status badges and success emphasis. Distinct from `semantic.success` (system success states).
- **Font**: **Inter** for headings + **Lexend** for body/UI. Lexend is drawn for reading fluency (wide apertures, tall x-height) which suits dense tables/forms; Inter keeps titles neutral so they don't compete with the data. Both SIL OFL 1.1, latin-ext (TR-safe). Loaded via Google Fonts in `apps/web/index.html`; tokens in `packages/ui/src/theme/designTokens.ts`; base `body` rule in `apps/web/src/index.css`. Mono = JetBrains Mono.
